          •     Continuous Data & Discrete Data / wLKav o;a; iy úúla; o;a;
                Continuous data represents variables that can take any value within a specified range, including
                fractions and decimals. It arises from measurements and is not limited to whole numbers. This type of
                data has an infinite number of possible values, as it is not restricted to distinct intervals.
                wLKav o;a; hkq Nd. iy oYu we;=¿j ksYaÑ; mrdihla ;=< ´kEu w.hla .; yels úp,Hhka
                ksfhdackh lrhs' tAjd mQ¾K ixLHd j,g iSud fkdfõ' fuu o;a; j¾.hg úhyels w.hka wkka;
                m%udKhla we;" ukao th fjkia ld, mrdihkag iSud fkdfõ'

                ex: Temperature, time, or distance
                Wod( WIaK;ajh" ld,h" ÿr

                Discrete data consists of distinct, separate values that can be counted individually. It is often associated
                with whole numbers and cannot take fractional or decimal values. Discrete data is usually derived
                from counting objects or occurrences.
                úúla; o;a; ;ks ;ksj .Kkh l< yels fjkia" fjku w.hkaf.ka iukaú; fõ' th fndfyda úg
                mQ¾K ixLHd iuÕ iïnkaO jk w;r Nd.sl fyda oYu w.hka fkdue;' úúla; o;a; idudkHfhka
                jHq;amkak jkafka jia;= fyda isÿùï .Kkh lsÍfuks'

    •   Big Data & Normal Data / uyd o;a; iy idudkH o;a;
        Big data is a term used to describe datasets so vast and complex that traditional data-processing
        methods are insufficient to handle them. These datasets often contain unstructured data like text,
        images, and videos, requiring specialized software and algorithms.
        uyd o;a; hkq idïm%odhsl o;a; ieliqï l%u tAjd yeisrùug m%udKj;a fkdjk ;rug w;súYd,

        úfYaIs; uDÿldx. iy we,af.dß;u wjYH jk mdG" rEm" iy ùäfhda jeks jHqy.; fkdjQ o;a;
        wvx.= fõ'

        Ex: Social media posts, sensor data from IoT devices
        Wod( iudc udOH m< lsÍï" IoT Wmdx.j,ska ,efnk ixfõol o;a;

        Normal data represents data that is manageable, often well-structured, and typically follows a
        predefined schema or format. It is limited in complexity and does not require advanced technologies
        for storage or analysis.
        idudkH o;a; uÕska l<ukdlrKh l< yels" fndfyda úg fyd¢ka jHqy.; jk iy idudkHfhka

    •   Metadata / mdro;a;
        Metadata is "data about data," providing descriptive information that helps identify, organize, and
        manage data. It describes the attributes, structure, or context of data, making it easier to locate, use, or
        understand.
        mdro;a; hkq o;a; y÷kd .ekSug" ixúOdkh lsÍug iy l<ukdlrKh lsÍug Wmldr jk

 2. Which of the following are good examples for batch processing?
    my; ljrla ldKav ieliqu (batch processing) i|yd fyd| WodyrK fõ o@
    A - a system that outputs the presently vacant vehicle parking space closest to a user

    B - a system that automatically backs up the files in a computer at the end of each day
        mß.Klhl f.dkq" iEu Èklu oji wjidkfha§" iajhxl%Shj Wmia:kh (backup) lrk moaO;shla
    C - a system that sorts the customer orders received during a day according to value
        ojila we;=<; ,o mdßfNda.sl wekjqï" jákdlfï wkqms<sfj<g ilik moaO;shla

    1) A only.           2) A and B only.    3) A and C only.     4) B and C only.     5) All of the above.
       A muKs.              A yd B muKs.        A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.         by; ish,a,u.

    Answer : 4)

    Batch processing & Real-time processing / ldKav ieliqï iy ;:H ld,Sk ieliqï
    Batch processing is a method of processing data where a group of tasks or transactions is collected over a
    period and then processed together in a single operation at a scheduled time. It does not provide immediate
    results.
    ldKav ieliSu hkq hï ld, mßÉfþohla ;=< ld¾hhka fyda .kqfokq iuQyhla tl;= lr miqj kshñ;
    fõ,djg tla fufyhqulska tlg ieliSfï o;a; ieliSfï l%uhls' th laIKsl m%;sM, ,nd fkdfoa'

    Ex: Payroll processing, where all employee data is processed at the end of a pay period.
    Wod( jegqma f,aLk ieliSu" jegqma ld, iSudj wjidkfha ish¨u fiajl o;a; ilikq ,efí'

    Real-time processing refers to the immediate processing of data and generation of results as data is
    inputted, enabling instant feedback or action. It is used in time-sensitive applications.
    ;:H ld,Sk ieliqï hkq laIKsl m%;sfmdaIKhla fyda l%shdjla il%Sh lrñka o;a; wdodkh lrk úg
    o;a; laIKslj ieliSu iy m%;sM, W;amdokh lsÍuhs' th ld, ixfõ§ fhÿïj, Ndú;d fõ'

    Ex: Credit card fraud detection during a transaction.
    Wod( .kqfokqjla w;r;=r fl%äÜ ldâ jxpd y÷kd .ekSu'

    Explanation of each statement / tla tla m%ldYh meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    Statement / m%ldYkh A
    This system provides real-time or near-real-time results because it needs to respond instantly to the user.
    Such a system falls under real-time processing, not batch processing.
    fuu moaO;sh mßYS,lhdg laIKslj m%;spdr oelaùug wjYH ksid ;;H ld,Sk fyda wdikak ;:H ld,Sk
    m%;sM, imhhs' tjeks moaO;shla ;:H ld,Sk ieliqï hg;g jefÜ" ldKav ieliSug fkdfõ'

    Statement / m%ldYkh B
    This process collects files throughout the day and executes the backup task at a scheduled time (e.g., end
    of the day). No immediate response is required, making it a good example of batch processing.
    fuu l%shdj,sh oji mqrd f.dkq tl;= lrk w;r Wmia: ld¾hh kshñ; fõ,djg l%shd;aul lrhs ^Wod'
    oji wjidkfha&' laIKsl m%;spdrhla wjYH fkdfõ" th ldKav ieliqï i|yd fyd| WodyrKhla njg
    m;a lrhs'

    Statement / m%ldYkh C
    This system processes all the orders together in a batch, typically at the end of the day or when triggered,
    and sorts them according to value. It doesn’t need to operate in real-time.
    fuu moaO;sh idudkHfhka oji wjidkfha§ fyda wjYH jQ úg" lKavdhula ;=< ish¨u weKjqï tlg
    ilihs" iy w.h wkqj tAjd j¾. lrhs' th ;:H ld,Skj l%shd;aul ùug wjYH fkdfõ'

    Accordingly, only B and C are true. So, the correct answer number here is 4.
    tA wkqj B yd C muKla i;H fõ' tuksid, fuys ksjerÈ ms<s;=re wxlh 4 fõ.

 4. A village of houses constructed mainly with the aid of a special equipment is nearing completion in the
    United States of America. This equipment has been used to construct the walls of the houses with the
    foundations and the roofs constructed in the traditional way. This equipment reduces the number of
    workers required for the construction process and has made the process faster and cheaper with minimized
    construction waste. What could be this special equipment?
    úfYaI WmlrKhl uQ,sl wdOdrfhka bÈflrekq ksjdi .ïudkhla weußld tlai;a ckmofha ksuùug
    wdikakh' ksjdij, ì;a;s fuu WmlrKh Ndú;fhka f.dvk.d we;s w;r" w;a;sjdrï iy jy,
    idïm%odhsl l%uhg f.dvk.d we;' bÈlsÍï l%shdj,shg wjYH fiajlhkaf.a .Kk fuu WmlrKh wvq
    lrk w;r l%shdj,sh blauka iy ,dNodhS fldg" bÈlsÍul§ we;sjk kdia;sh o wju lr we;' fuu
    úfYaI WmlrKh l=ula úh yels o@
     1) a digitizer / ixLHdxllhla                      2) a large 3D printer / úYd, ;%sudK uqølhla
     3) a plotter / ,l=Kq lrkhla                       4) a pointing device / oelajqï Wmdx.hla
     5) a joystick / fufyhqï háhla

     Answer : 2)
     A large 3D printer is increasingly being used in the construction industry to create structures such as walls
     and other parts of buildings by depositing material layer by layer.
     úYd, ;%sudK uqøK hka;%hla bÈlsÍï lafIa;%fha jeä jeäfhka Ndú;d lrñka f.dvke.s,sj, ì;a;s iy
     wfkl=;a fldgia jeks jHqyhka ia:rfhka ia:r ;ekam;a lsÍfuka ks¾udKh lrhs'

     This technology / fuu ;dlaIKh:
     −   Automates significant portions of the construction process.
         bÈlsÍï l%shdj,sfha ie,lsh hq;= fldgia iajhxl%Sh lrhs'
     −   Reduces the need for labor.
         fiajl wjYH;dj wvq lrhs'
     −   Decreases material waste through precise material deposition.
         ksYaÑ; øjH ;ekam;a ùu yryd wmøjH wvq lrhs'
     −   Speeds up the construction process while reducing costs.
         msßjeh wvq lrk w;ru bÈlsÍï l%shdj,sh fõ.j;a lrhs'

     Why the other options are incorrect / fjk;a úl,am jerÈ jkafka wehs(

     Option / úl,amh 1)
     A digitizer converts analog information into digital form. It is used for input purposes (e.g., drawing or
     design) but cannot physically construct walls.
     ixLHdxllhla m%;siu f;dr;=re wxls; wdldrhg mßj¾;kh lrhs' th wdodk wruqKq i|yd Ndú;d
     lrhs ^Wod( we£u fyda ie,iqï lsÍu& kuq;a fN!;slj ì;a;s ;eksh fkdyel'

     Option / úl,amh 3)
     A plotter is a printing device that draws graphics or designs, mainly for engineering drawings. It cannot
     be used for physical construction.
     ,l=Kqlrkh hkq m%Odk jYfhka bxðfkare Ñ;% i|yd Ñ;%l fyda fudaia;r w¢k uqøK WmlrKhls' th
     fN!;sl bÈlsÍï i|yd Ndú;d l< fkdyel'

     Option / úl,amh 4)
     A pointing device, such as a mouse or trackpad, is used to interact with a computer interface. It has no role
     in physical construction.
     mß.Kl w;=reuqyqK;la iuÕ wka;¾l%shd lsÍug uQislhla fyda g%ela mEâ jeks oelaùfï Wmdx.hla
     Ndú;d lrhs' fN!;sl f.dvke.Sfï§ tys ld¾hNdrhla fkdue;'

     A joystick is primarily a control device for navigation or gaming and has no application in the construction
     of houses.
     fufyhqï háh hkq uQ,sl jYfhka ixpd,kh fyda l%Svd i|yd md,k Wmdx.hla jk w;r ksjdi

    Answer : 3)

    The northbridge is responsible for handling high-speed components, such as the CPU, memory (RAM),
    ROM and transferring data between hard disk and RAM. Located close to the CPU on the motherboard,
    the northbridge uses a high-speed bus for direct data transfer. In modern systems, many of the northbridge's
    functions, like memory and ROM communication, have been integrated into the CPU itself.
    CPU" u;lh (RAM), ROM jeks wêfõ.S Wmdx. yeisrùu iy oDV äialh iy RAM w;r o;a;
    yqjudre lsÍu i|yd Northbridge j.lSu orhs' ujq mqjrefõ CPU tlg wdikakj msysgd we;s
    Northbridge iDcq o;a; yqjudrej i|yd wêfõ.S nihla Ndú;d lrhs' kùk moaO;s ;=<" u;lh iy
    ROM ikaksfõokh jeks Northbridge ys fndfyda ld¾hhka CPU fj;u tAldnoaO lr we;'

    The southbridge, on the other hand, manages slower components and peripherals. These include USB
    ports, audio ports, network interfaces, and storage devices like hard drives and solid-state drives. It serves
    as the controller for input/output (I/O) operations and peripherals, ensuring these devices communicate
    effectively with the CPU. Unlike the northbridge, the southbridge is located further away from the CPU
    as it handles lower-speed tasks. In newer systems, the southbridge’s functionality has been combined into
    a single chip known as the Platform Controller Hub (PCH).
    wfkla w;g" southbridge ukao.dó ixrpl iy m¾hka; l<ukdlrKh lrhs' fïjdg USB flfjks"
    Y%jH flfjks" cd, w;=reuqyqK;a iy ùäfhda flfjks we;=<;a fõ' th wdodk$m%;sodk (I/O) fufyhqï
    iy m¾hka; i|yd md,lh f,i l%shd lrhs" fuu Wmdx. CPU iuÕ M,odhS f,i ikaksfõokh lsÍu

    w¨;ajeähd lsÍulska miqj fïi mr.Klhla oDV äialfhka b;d fõ.fhka wdrïN ^boot& jk nj
    mqoa.,fhla ksÍlaIKh lrhs' w¨;ajeähdfõ È my; ljrla isÿ lrkakg we;s o@
    1) Decreasing RAM and reinstalling the operating system
       RAM wvq lr fufyhqï moaO;sh kej; ia:dmkh lsÍu'
    2) Formatting the hard disk only
       oDv äialh wdlD;slrKh ^Format& lsÍu muKla
    3) Installing a new CD drive only
       w¨;a CD Odjlhla msysgqùu ^Install& muKla
    4) Replacing the small fan inside the computer only
       mß.Klh ;=< we;s l=vd mxldj ^fan& wdfoaY lsÍu muKla
    5) Replacing the hard disk with a Solid-state Drive (SSD) and reinstalling the operating system
       oDv äialh fjkqjg >k ;;a;aj Odjlhla wdfoaY lr fufyhqï moaO;sh kej; ia:dmkh lsÍu

    Answer : 5)

    Booting
    The process of starting up a computer and loading the operating system into memory from a storage device
    (ex: hard disk or SSD). Faster booting means the system starts more quickly after being turned on.
    mß.Klhla wdrïN lsÍu iy .nvd Wmdx.hlska fufyhqï moaO;sh u;lhg megùfï l%shdj,sh ^Wod(
    oDV ;eáh fyda SSD&' fõ.j;a wdrïNhla hkq moaO;sh il%sh lsÍfuka miq blaukska wdrïN ùuhs'

    Hard Disk / oDV ;eáh
    A traditional data storage device (HDD) using spinning magnetic platters to store and retrieve digital
    information. It is slower compared to modern storage technologies like SSDs.
    wxls; f;dr;=re .nvd lsÍu iy ,nd .ekSu i|yd lerflk pqïnl ;eá Ndú;d lrk iïm%odhsl
    o;a; .nvd lsÍfï Wmdx.hls (HDD) SSD jeks kùk .nvd ;dlaIKhkag idfmalaIj ukao.dó fõ'

    RAM (Random Access Memory / iiïNdú m%fõY u;lh)
    Temporary memory used by the computer to perform tasks. More RAM generally means better
    multitasking and faster performance, but it doesn’t directly affect boot speed.
    ;djld,sl u;lh ld¾hhka bgq lsÍu i|yd mß.Klh Ndú;d lrhs' jeä RAM hkq idudkHfhka jvd
    fyd| nyq-ld¾hhla iy fõ.j;a l%shdldß;ajhla woyia lrhs' kuq;a th werUqï fõ.h fl,skau
    n,mdkafka ke;'

    Formatting / wdlD;slrK
    The process of preparing a storage device like a hard disk by erasing all data and setting up a new file
    system. It doesn’t inherently improve speed unless combined with other actions.
    ish¨u o;a; uld oud kj f.dkq moaO;shla ieliSfuka oDV ;eáhla jeks .nvd Wmdx.hla ilia lsÍfï
    l%shdj,shhs' th fjk;a l%shd iuÕ ixl,kh ù we;akï ñi iycfhkau fõ.h jeä ÈhqKq fkdlrhs'

    CD Drive
    An optical drive used for reading or writing CDs. Installing a new CD drive has no impact on boot speed.
    ixhqla; ;eá lshùu fyda ,sùu i|yd Ndú;d lrk oDYH Odjlhla' kj CD Odjlhla ia:dmkh lsÍu
    werUqï fõ.h flfrys n,mEula ke;'

    Fan / úÿ,s mxldj

    addresses thermal issues but doesn’t affect speed.

    lsÍfuka ;dm .eg¿ úi|k kuq;a fõ.hg n,mdkafka ke;'

    Solid-State Drive / >k-;;aj Odjl (SSD)

    reading and writing data.

     according to the ASCII code. The binary representation of No! is kept blank.
     my; j.=fõ fojeks iy ;=kajeks fma<sj, bx.%Sis jpk folla iy ASCII fla;hg wkqj tAjdfha oaúuh
     ksrEmKhka we;' No! ys oaúuh ksrEmKh ysiaj ;nd we;'

         no                                1101110 1101111

         No!

     What is the correct replacement for the blank?
     ysia;ekg iqÿiq wdfoaYlh l=ulao@

     1) 0100001 1001110 1101111                        2) 1001110 0100001 1101111
     3) 1001110 1101111 0100001                        4) 1101110 0100001 1101111
     5) 1101110 1101111 0100001

     Answer : 3)

     BCD (Binary-Coded Decimal), EBCDIC (Extended Binary Coded Decimal Interchange Code), ASCII
     (American Standard Code for Information Interchange), and Unicode are encoding systems used to
     represent characters in computers.
     BCD (Binary-Coded Decimal), EBCDIC (Extended Binary Coded Decimal Interchange Code, ASCII
     (American Standard Code for Information Interchange) iy hqksfldaâ hkq mß.Klj, wlaIr ksrEmKh
     lsÍug Ndú;d lrk fla;SlrK moaO;s fõ'

     •    BCD is an early encoding method primarily for numeric data, using 4-bit binary codes to represent
          decimal digits (0–9).
          BCD hkq oYu b,lalï (0–9) ksfhdackh lsÍu i|yd ^4-bit& oaúuh fla; Ndú;d lrk uQ,sl
          jYfhka ixLHd;aul o;a; i|yd uq,a fla;SlrK l%uhls'

     •    EBCDIC, developed by IBM, is an 8-bit character, supporting 256 characters, including letters,
          numbers, and special symbols.
          IBM úiska jeä ÈhqKq lrk ,o EBCDIC hkq wl=re" wxl iy úfYaI ixfla; we;=¿j wlaIr
          256 lg iyh olajk wlaIr fla;k moaO;shls'

     •    ASCII, widely used in modern systems, is a 7-bit encoding standard that represents 128 characters,
          including English letters, numbers, and control codes, later extended to 8 bits (Extended ASCII) for
          256 characters.
          kùk moaO;sj, nyq,j Ndú;d jk ASCII hkq bx.%Sis wl=re" wxl iy md,k fla; we;=¿j wlaIr
          128& la ksfhdackh lrk 7-ìgq fla;SlrK m%ñ;shls" miqj wlaIr 256 i|yd ìgq 8 (Extended
          ASCII) olajd §¾> lrk ,§'

     •    Unicode is the most comprehensive encoding system, supporting over 143,000 characters from
          various languages, symbols, and scripts worldwide. It uses variable-length encoding formats like UTF-
          8, UTF-16 and UTF-32, making it the global standard for
          hqksfldaâ hkq f,dj mqrd úúO NdId" ixfla; iy ial%smaÜ j,ska wlaIr 143"000lg jvd iyh olajk
          jvd;a úia;S¾K fla;SlrK moaO;shhs' th UTF-8" UTF-16 iy UTF-32 jeks úp,H-È. fla;SlrK
          wdlD;s Ndú;d lrhs" th f.da,Sh m%ñ;sh njg m;a lrhs'

     The table provides binary representations for ASCII-encoded characters of two words. The task is to find
     the binary representation for No!.
     j.=j jpk foll ASCII-fla;kh l< wlaIr i|yd oaúuh ksrEmKhka imhhs ld¾hh jkafka No!
     i|yd oaúuh ksrEmKh fidhd .ekSuhs'

 15. Which of the following are correct regarding the Process Control Block (PCB)?
     l%shdhk md,k LKavh (PCB) iïnkaOfhka my; ljrla ksjerÈ fõ o@
    A- It is a data structure used by the operating system to manage information about a process.
       th l%shdhkhl (process) f;dr;=re l<uKdlrKh lsÍu ioyd fufyhqï moaO;sh úiska Ndú;d
       lrkq ,nk o;a; jHqyhls'
    B- It is created during the compilation of a program.
       th l%uf,aLhla iïmdokfha (compilation) § iEfoa'
    C- The Program Counter values of two PCBs can be the same.
       PCB foll Program Counter w.hka tl iudk úh yels h'

    1) A only.          2) A and B only.    3) A and C only.     4) B and C only.    5) All of the above.
       A muKs.             A yd B muKs.        A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.        by; ish,a,u.

    Answer : 3)

    Process Control Block / l%shdhk md,k LKavh(

    •   A data structure maintained by the operating system for each process.
        tla tla l%shdhkh i|yd fufyhqï moaO;sh úiska mj;ajdf.k hkq ,nk o;a; jHqyhls'
    •   Stores essential information like process ID, program counter (PC), CPU registers, memory
        management details, process state, and more.
        l%shdhk yÿkajkh" jevigyka .Klh" CPU frðia;r" u;l l<ukdlrK úia;r" l%shdhk ;;a;ajh
        jeks w;HjYH f;dr;=re fndfyduhla .nvd lrhs'

    Program Counter / l%uf,aL .Klh(

    •   A register that holds the memory address of the next instruction to be executed by the process.
        l%shdhkh u.ska l%shd;aul l< hq;= ó<Õ Wmfofiys u;l ,smshkh wvx.= frðia;rhls'
    •   Keeps track of where the process is in its instruction sequence.
        l%shdhkh tys Wmfoia wkqms<sfjf,ys l=uk ia:dkfha isákafka o hkak ms<sno igyka ;nd.kS'

    Explanation of each statement / tla tla m%ldYh meyeÈ,s lsÍu(
    Statement / m%ldYh A – Correct / ksjerÈhs
    As stated above, PCB is a data structure maintained by the operating system that holds information about
    a process.
    by; i|yka l< mßÈ" PCB hkq l%shdj,shla ms<sn| f;dr;=re wvx.= fufyhqï moaO;sh úiska
    mj;ajdf.k hkq ,nk o;a; jHqyhls'

    mßj¾;kh lsÍuhs'

     Computer Boot Process / mß.Kl werUqï l%shdj,sh(
     When a computer is powered on, the BIOS (Basic Input/Output System) initializes hardware and loads
     the operating system from secondary storage into memory. The OS then takes control of the computer.
     mß.Klhla n,.kajk úg§" BIOS ^uQ,sl wdodk$m%;sodk moaO;sh& u.ska oDVdx. wdrïN lr fufyhqï
     moaO;sh oaù;Sl .nvdfõ isg u;lhg mQrKh lrhs' túg OS tl u.ska mß.Klfha md,kh ,nd .kS'

     Multi-User Operating Systems / nyq-mßYS,l fufyhqï moaO;s(
     A multi-user OS allows multiple users to access the computer simultaneously. Each user’s processes are
     managed separately but can share system resources like the CPU.
     nyq-mßYS,l fufyhqï moaO;shla uÕska mßYS,lhska lsysm fofkl=g tljr mß.Klhg m%fõY ùug
     bv i,ihs' tla tla mßYS,lhdf.a l%shdhk fjk fjku l<ukdlrKh lrk kuq;a tAjdg CPU tl
     jeks moaO;s iïm;a fnod .; yel'

     Process Execution and Scheduling / l%shdhkh l%shd;aul lsÍu iy kshu lsÍu(
     The OS uses process scheduling to switch between processes to ensure fair resource allocation. It does
     this by alternating between different processes (e.g., Sama’s web browser, text editor, and Rani’s web
     browser), inserting its own control (OS-related tasks) in between.
     idOdrKj iïm;a fjka lsÍu iy;sl lsÍu i|yd l%shdhk w;r udre ùug OS tl u.ska l%shdhk
     kshulrK Ndú;d lrhs' tu.ska ;ukaf.a md,k l%shdjka ^OS-wdY%s; ld¾hhka& fuu udre ùï w;rg
     we;=<;a lrñka úúO l%shdhk w;r udre fjñka ^Wod'" idudf.a fjí w;rslaiqj" mdG ixialdrlh iy
     rdKSf.a fjí w;rslaiqj& fuu l%shdj,sh isÿ lrhs'

    Execution Sequence / l%shd;aul lsÍfï wkqms<sfj,(

    The processor follows a sequence of execution steps:
    ilikh l%shd;aul lsÍfï mshjr wkqms<sfj,la wkq.ukh lrhs(

    −   First, the BIOS initializes the system and loads the OS.
        m<uqj" BIOS uÕska moaO;sh wdrïN lr fufyhqï moaO;sh mQrKh lrhs'
    −   Next, user processes are executed in an interleaved fashion, with the OS managing the transitions.
        ó<Õg" OS uÕska ixl%dka;s l<ukdlrKh lsÍu iu.u mßYs,l l%shdhk wka;¾ iïnkaê;
        wdldrfhka l%shd;aul fõ'

    Incorrect Sequences / jerÈ wkqms<sfj,(
    Any sequence where the BIOS appears after the OS or user processes is invalid because the BIOS only
    runs during boot and does not appear again after the OS is loaded.
    OS fyda mßYS,l l%shdhkhkaf.ka miqj BIOS Èiajk ´kEu wkqms<sfj,la j,x.= fkdfõ" ukao BIOS
    wdrïNfha§ muKla l%shd;aul jk w;r OS tl mQrKh jQ miq kej; fkdfmfka'

    •   The BIOS initializes the system, the OS is loaded, and processes are executed alternately.
        BIOS uÕska moaO;sh wdrïN lrhs" fufyhqï moaO;sh mQrkh lrkq ,efí" iy l%shdhk yqjudre
        fjñka l%shd;aul fõ'

        sequence correctly follows the behavior of a multi-user operating system.

        yqjudrej w;r th Èiafõ' fuu wkqms<sfj, nyq-mßYS,l fufyhqï moaO;shl yeisÍu ksjerÈj
        wkq.ukh lrhs'

    Sequence / wkqms<sfj, (2) – Invalid / j,x.= fkdfõ

    •   While the sequence might seem plausible, the OS is missing during several context switches (ex:
        between Sama’s web browser process and Sama’s text editor process).
        wkqms<sfj, ms<s.; yels njla fmfkkakg ;snqK;a" ikao¾N iaúp lsysmhl§ OS tl fkdfmkS
        f.dia we; ^Wod( idudf.a fjí w;rslaiq l%shdj,sh iy idudf.a mdG ixialdrl l%shdj,sh w;r&'
    •   The OS must always intervene during process transitions, which is not reflected here, making this

        l%shdhk ixl%dka;s j,§ fufyhqï moaO;sh ieuúgu ueÈy;a úh hq;= w;r" th fuys ms<sìUq fkdjk
        w;r" fuu wkqms<sfj, wj,x.= fõ'

        fufyhqï moaO;sh wdrïN jQ miq BIOS kej; fkdfmfka'

        OS úiska iEu ikao¾N iaúphlau l<ukdlrKh l< hq;= w;r" fuu wkqms<sfj<g m%udKj;a OS
        ys iïnkaOhla fkdue;'

 17. Each block of a disk is 512 bytes. When a file of size 1959 bytes is stored on that disk, how many bytes
     allocated to the file would be wasted?
     äialhl lÜáhla (block) nhsg 512 la fõ' nhsg 1959 l úYd,;ajhlska hq;a f.dkqjla tu äialfha
     wdph l< úg" tu f.dkqjg fjka lrk ,o nhsg fldmuK ixLHdjla wmf;a hkq we;s o@

     1) 89                 2) 423                 3) 512                 4) 601                 5) 1447

     Answer : 1)

     Disk Storage Blocks:
     Hard drives and other storage devices divide data into blocks of a fixed size (e.g., 512 bytes in this
     question). A block is the smallest unit of storage that the disk can handle.
     oDV ;eá iy wfkl=;a .nvd Wmdx. o;a; ia:djr m%udKfha lÜá j,g fnohs ^Wod( fuu m%Yakfha
     nhsg 512ls&' ìf,dla tlla hkq ;eáhg yeisrúh yels l=vdu .nvd tAllhhs'

     Files are stored in these blocks, and even if the file size doesn’t perfectly fit into the blocks, the remaining
     unused space in the last block still has to be allocated to the file, leading to wasted space.
     f.dkq fuu lÜá j, .nvd lrk w;r" f.dkqfõ m%udKh ìf,dla j,g iïmq¾Kfhkau fkd.e<mqko"
     wjidk lÜáfha b;sßj we;s Ndú;hg fkd.;a bv ;ju;a f.dkqjg fjka lsÍug isÿ jk w;r th
     kdia;shg ;=vq fohs'

     File Size and Block Calculation:
     The size of the file determines how many blocks are needed.
     f.dkqfõ úYd,;ajh wkqj fldmuK lÜá m%udKhla wjYHoehs ;SrKh lrkq ,nhs'

     To calculate the number of blocks required, the file size is divided by the block size and rounded up to the
     next whole number, as partial blocks cannot exist.
     wjYH lÜáixLHdj .Kkh lsÍu i|yd" f.dkqfõ m%udKh ìf,dla tll m%udKfhka fnokq ,nk w;r"
     w¾O ìf,dla lÜá fkdyels neúka" B<Õ mQ¾K ixLHdj olajd ms<s;=r jghd lÜá ixLHdj ,nd .kq
     ,efí'

     Wasted Space:
     Wasted space is the difference between the allocated block space (total space of all blocks used) and the
     actual file size. This occurs in the last block if the file does not completely fill it.
     kdia;s jQ bv hkq fjka lrk ,o lÜá wjldYh ^Ndú;d lrk ,o ish¨u lÜáj, iïmQ¾K bv& iy
     i;H f.dkq m%udKh w;r fjkihs' f.dkqj iïmQ¾Kfhkau lÜáhl m%udKhg .e<fmkafka fkdue;s kï
     fuh wjidk ìf,dla tl ;=< isÿ fõ'

     Explanation of the answer / ms<s;=r meyeÈ,s lsÍu

     File size = 1959 bytes
     Block size = 512 bytes

     •   Step 1:
         Calculating the number of blocks required.
         wjYH íf,dla fldgia ixLHdj .Kkh lsÍu'
         Number of blocks (lÜá fldgia .Kk)= File size (f.dkq m%udKh) / The size of one block (ìf,dla
         fldgil m%udKh)
         Number of blocks (lÜá fldgia .Kk) = 1959 / 512
         Number of blocks (lÜá fldgia .Kk) = 3.82
         Number of blocks (lÜá fldgia .Kk) = 4 (rounded)
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -21-                                          #ictfromabc


     •   Step 2:
         Calculating the total space allocated.
         fjka lr we;s uq¿ bv m%udKh .Kkh lsÍu'
         Each block is 512 bytes, and 4 blocks are used:
         iEu lÜáhlu nhsg 512 la jk w;r tjeks lÜá 4 la Ndú;d fõ(
         Total space allocated (Ndú;d ú we;s uq¿ bv)= 4×512 = 2048

     •   Step 3:
         Calculating the wasted space.
         wmf;a hk bv m%udKh .Kkh lsÍu'
         The actual file size is 1959 bytes, so the wasted space is:
         i;H f.dkq m%udKh nhsg 1959 fõ" tneúka wmf;a hk bv m%udKh(
         Wasted space (wmf;a hk bv m%udKh) = Total space allocated (Ndú;d ú we;s uq¿ bv)−File size
         (f.dkq m%udKh)
         Wasted space (wmf;a hk bv m%udKh) = 2048 – 1959
         Wasted space (wmf;a hk bv m%udKh) = 89 bytes

 18. Which of the given statements (I, II, III) are true with respect to the following two diagrams?
     my; rEm fol iïnkaOfhka § we;s ljr j.ka;s (I, II, III) ksjerÈ o@
     (Note: V - Voltage, t - time)
     ^ie'hq' V - fjda,aàh;dj t - ld,h&

     I - A depicts an analog signal.
         A m%;siu (analog) ix{djla ksrEmKh lrhs'
     II - B depicts a digital signal.
          B wxls; (digital) ix{djla ksrEmKh lrhs'
     III - B is a digitized version of A.
           B hkq A ys ixLHdxlkh (digitize) l< iajrEmhhs'


     1) I only.          2) II only.         3) III only.       4) I and III only.    5) All of the above.
        I muKs.             II muKs.            III muKs.          I yd III muKs.        by; ish,a,u.
     Answer : 5)
     Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu:

     Analog Signals / m%;siu ix{d(
     Analog signals vary continuously over time and represent data in a smooth manner.
     m%;siu ix{d ld,h;a iuÕ ika;;slj fjkia jk w;r o;a; iqug wdldrhlska ksfhdackh lrhs'
     Example / WodyrKh (
     The waveform labeled A in the diagram is an analog signal because it is smooth and continuous.
     rEm igyfka A f,i f,an,a lr we;s ;rx. wdlD;sh iqug yd ika;;sl neúka th m%;siu ix{djlafõ'
     Digital Signals / wxls; ix{d(
     Digital signals vary discretely within a specific range of energy levels, taking on fixed values (steps).
     ia:djr w.hka ^mshjr& ,nd .ksñka ksYaÑ; Yla;s uÜgï mrdihla ;=< wxls; ix{d úúla;j fjkiafõ'
     While digital systems typically use binary levels (ex: 0 and 1), they can theoretically employ multiple
     levels to encode more information. However, practical constraints like noise and technological limitations
     make this difficult.
     wxls; moaO;s idudkHfhka oaúuh uÜgï ^Wod( 0 iy 1& Ndú;d lrk w;r" jeä f;dr;=re m%udKhla
     fla;kh lsÍug kHdhd;aulj uÜgï lsysmhla jqjo fhdod .; yelsh' flfia fj;;a" f>daIdj iy

    Example / WodyrK (
    The waveform labeled B in the diagram is a digital signal, as it consists of discrete steps.
    rEm igyfka B f,i f,an,a lr we;s ;rx. wdlD;sh wxls; ix{djla jk w;r th úúla; mshjr
    j,ska iukaú; fõ'

    Statement / m%ldYh I - Correct / ksjerÈhs
    Diagram A represents a continuous signal that varies smoothly over time, which is characteristic of an
    analog signal. Analog signals are not discrete and can take on any value within a range, as shown in the
    continuous nature of A's waveform. This confirms that A depicts an analog signal.
    rEm igyk A hkq m%;siu ix{djl ,laIKhla jk ld,h;a iuÕ iqug f,i fjkia jk wLKav
    ix{djla ksfhdackh lrhs' wxls; ix{d úúla; fkdjk w;r A ys ;rx. wdlD;sfha wLKav iajNdjfha

    ;yjqre fõ'

    of digital signals. Digital signals operate on distinct values, often represented as 0s and 1s, and the stepped
    nature of B's waveform confirms that it is a digital signal.
    rEm igyk B iukaú; jkafka wxls; ix{dj, uQ,sl .=Kdx.hla jk oaúuh ;;ajhka ksfhdackh lrk
    úúla; mshjr fyda uÜgï j,skah' ixLHdxl ix{d fjkia w.hka u; l%shd lrhs" fndfyda úg 0s iy

 19. Which of the following is correct regarding parity bits?
     iu;a (parity) ìgq iïnkaOfhka my; ljrla ksjerÈ o@
    1) The parity bit in a set of bits is adjusted after that set of bits is communicated.
       ìgq iuqyhl iu;aj ìgqj" tu ìgq iuqyh ikaksfõokfhka miqj yß.eiaifjhs'

    2) The parity bit for a set of bits is selected to ensure the total number of 1-bits in the set is either even
       or odd. / ìgq iuqyhl iu;aj ìgqj f;dard.efkkqfha tu iuqyfha uq¿ 1-ìgq .Kk brÜfÜ fyda
       T;af;a jk mßÈh'

    3) The transmission speed of a communication is increased by parity bits.
       ikaksfõokhl iïfm%aIK fõ.h iu;aj ìgq u.ska jeä flf¾'

    4) Parity bits are added after a communication to correct errors.
       jerÈ ksjerÈ lsÍug" ikaksfõokhlska miqj iu;aj ìgq tl;= flf¾'

    5) Encryption needed in a data transmission is provided by parity bits.
       o;a; iïfm%aIKhlg wjYH .=ma; fla;kh (encryption) iu;aj ìgq u.ska iemfha'

    Answer : 2)

    Parity Bits / iu;aj ìgq(
    A parity bit is an additional bit added to a data set to detect errors during transmission by ensuring the total
    number of 1-bits in the data follows a specific parity (either even or odd).
    iu;aj ìgq hkq o;a;j, we;s uq¿ 1ys ìgq .Kk ksYaÑ; iudk;djla ^brÜfÜ fyda T;af;a& wkq.ukh
    lrk nj iy;sl lr .ekSfuka iïfm%aIKh w;r;=r fodaI y÷kd .ekSug o;a; lÜg,hlg tl;=
    lrk ,o w;sf¾l ìgqjls'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -23-                                          #ictfromabc


    Error Detection / fodaI y÷kd.ekSu(
    Parity bits are primarily used for detecting errors, not correcting them. If an error is detected, the system
    typically requests retransmission.
    iu;aj ìgq uQ,sl jYfhka Ndú;d lrkqfha fodaI y÷kd .ekSug fõ' fodaY ksjerÈ lsÍug Ndú; fkdfõ'
    fodaIhla wkdjrKh jqjfyd;a" moaO;sh idudkHfhka kej; iïfm%aIKhla b,a,d isà'

    Parity Types / iu;d j¾.:

    •   Even Parity: Ensures the total number of 1-bits in the data (including the parity bit) is even.
        brÜfÜ iu;dj( o;a;j, we;s uq¿ 1ys ìgq .Kk ^iu;aj ìgq we;=¿j& brÜfÜ nj iy;sl lrhs'

    •   Odd Parity: Ensures the total number of 1-bits in the data (including the parity bit) is odd.
        T;af;a iu;dj( o;a;j, we;s uq¿ 1ys ìgq .Kk ^iu;aj ìgq we;=¿j& T;af;a nj iy;sl lrhs'

    Practical Limitations / m%dfhda.sl iSudjka(
    Parity bits cannot detect errors if two or more bits are flipped in the data. They are also unrelated to
    encryption and can add minor overhead, reducing transmission speed.
    o;a;j, ìgq folla fyda jeä .Kkla udre jqjfyd;a iu;aj ìgqj,g fodaI y÷kd.; fkdyel' tAjd
    fla;khg o iïnkaO ke;s w;r iïfm%aIK fõ.h wvq lrñka iq¿ w;sßla; ld¾hhla tl;= l< yel'

    Therefore, the correct answer is option 2).

     Network) line?
     fmdÿ iaúp ÿrl:k cd, u.lska m%;siu (analog) ix{djla ,o úg fudvuhlska l=ula isÿ fõ o@
    1) It amplifies the signal for better clarity.

    2) It compresses the signal for storage.
       wdphkh (storage) ioyd th ix{dj iïmSvkh (compress) lrhs'
    3) It demodulates the signal back into digital form.
       th ixLHdj h,s wxls; (digital) iajrEmhg úuQ¾ckh (demodulate) lrhs'
    4) It encrypts the signal for security.

    5) It modulates the signal further for transmission.

    Answer : 3 )

    Public Switched Telephone Network / fmdÿ iaúp ÿrl:k cd,
    The Public Switched Telephone Network (PSTN) is the traditional network for voice communication. It
    uses analog signals over landlines and circuit-switched technology to establish calls between users. It
    serves as the backbone for global voice communication.
    fmdÿ iaúp ÿrl:k cd, (PSTN) hkq yv ikaksfõokh i|yd jQ iïm%odhsl cd,hhs' th mßYS,lhka

    Ndú;d lrhs' fuh f.da,Sh yv ikaksfõokh i|yd fld÷ kdráh f,i fiajh lrhs'

    Modem / uQ¾clh
    A modem is a device that converts signals between analog and digital formats to enable communication
    over different mediums (ex: telephone lines).
    fudvuhla hkq úúO udOH yryd ^Wod( ÿrl:k ud¾.& ikaksfõokh il%Sh lsÍu i|yd m%;siu iy
    wxls; wdlD;s w;r ix{d mßj¾;kh lrk WmlrKhls'

    •   Modulation / uQ¾ckh

        lrhs'

    wxls; wdlD;shla njg mßj¾;kh lrñka ix{dj úuQ¾ckh lrhs'

        Secures the signal but is unrelated to signal conversion.
        ix{dj iqrlaIs; lrk kuq;a ix{d mßj¾;khg iïnkaO fkdfõ'

    The modem demodulates the analog signal back into digital form for computer processing. So, the correct

 21. Which of the following best describes a switch in a network?
     cd,hl we;s iaúphla jvd;a fyd¢ka úia;r lrkafka my; l=uk m%ldYfhka o@
    1) It amplifies data signals for clearer transmission.
       jvd;a meyeÈ,s iïfm%aIKh i|yd th o;a; ix{d úia;drKh (amplify) lrhs'
    2) It always broadcasts all incoming data to every device in the network.
       th iEuúgu msg;ska tk ish¨ o;a; cd,fha we;s iEu Wmdx.hlgu úldYh (broadcast) lrhs'
    3) It compresses data for more efficient transmission.
       jvd;a ld¾hlaIu iïfm%aIKh i|yd th o;a; iïmSvkh (compress) lrhs'
    4) It directs data only to the specific device for which the data is intended.
       o;a; wfmalaYs; Wmdx.hg muKla th o;a; fhduq lrhs'
    5) It stores data for future processing.
       wkd.; ieliqï (processing) i|yd th o;a; wdph (store) lrhs'

    Answer : 4)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    A switch is a networking device that operates primarily at the data link layer (Layer 2) of the OSI model.
    Switches are essential for efficient and secure communication in networks.
    iaúphla hkq OSI wdlD;sfha o;a; iïnkaOl ia:rfha (Layer 2) uQ,sl jYfhka l%shd;aul jk cd,lrK
    Wmdx.hls' cd, ;=< ld¾hlaIu iy wdrlaIs; ikaksfõokh i|yd iaúphka w;HjYH fõ'

    Its main functions include / tys m%odk ld¾hhka j,g we;=,;a jkafka(

    •   Directing data / o;a; fufyhùu
        A switch reads the MAC address of incoming data packets and forwards the data only to the specific
        device whose MAC address matches the destination address. This ensures efficient communication in
        the network.
        iaúphla uÕska meñfKk o;a; melÜ j, MAC ,smskh lshjk w;r MAC ,smskh .ukdka;
        ,smskhg .e<fmk ksYaÑ; Wmdx.hg muKla o;a; fhduq lrhs' fuh cd,h ;=< ld¾hlaIu
        ikaksfõokh iy;sl lrhs'

    •   Not broadcasting / úldYkh fkdfõ
        Unlike a hub, a switch does not send data to all devices in the network. It is more selective, reducing
        unnecessary traffic.
        kdôhla fuka fkdj" iaúphla cd,fha ish¨u Wmdx. fj; o;a; hjkafka ke;' th jvd;a f;dard
        fírd" wkjYH .ukd.ukh wvq lrhs'

    Why other options are incorrect / fjk;a úl,am jerÈ wehs(

    Option / úl,am 1)
    This is the function of a repeater or signal booster, not a switch.
    fuh mqk¾fhdaclhl fyda ix{d j¾Olhl l%shdldß;ajh ñi iaúphla fkdfõ'

    Option / úl,am 2)
    This is the behavior of a hub, not a switch. A hub sends data to all devices, whereas a switch sends it only
    to the intended recipient.
    fuh flakaøhl yeisÍu ñi iaúphla fkdfõ' kdôhla ish¨u Wmdx. fj; o;a; hjk w;r iaúphla
    uÕska th hjkafka wfmalaIs; ,nkakdg muKS'

    Option / úl,am 3)
    A switch does not compress data. Data compression is typically handled by software or specific network
    devices designed for compression.
    iaúphla o;a; iïmSvkh fkdlrhs' o;a; iïmSvkh idudkHfhka yiqrejkafka iïmSvkh i|yd ks¾udKh
    lr we;s uDÿldx. fyda úfYaIs; cd, Wmdx. u.sks'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -26-                                           #ictfromabc


    Option / úl,am 5)
    Switches do not store data long-term. They only temporarily hold data in their memory (buffer) while it is
    being processed and forwarded.
    iaúphka È.= ld,Skj o;a; .nvd fkdlrhs' Tjqka o;a; ilia lr bÈßhg hjk w;r;=r Tjqkaf.a
    u;lfha ^n*rh& ;djld,slj muKla r|jd ;nd .kS'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 4).
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 4) fõ'


 22. How many usable host addresses are available in the 192.168.100.0/27 IP address block ?
     192.168.100.0/27 IP fhduq ldKavfha Ndú;hg .; yels i;aldrl fhduq fldmuK mj;So ?

    1) 16                2) 30                 3) 32                 4) 62                 5) 64

    Answer : 2)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    An IP address block with a /27 subnet mask means that the first 27 bits of the IP address are reserved for
    the network portion, leaving the remaining 5 bits for the host portion.
    /27 Wmcd, wdjrKhla iys; IP ,smsk íf,dla tlla hkq IP ,smskfha m<uq ìgq 27 cd, fldgi i|yd
    fjka lr we;s w;r b;sß ìgq 5 Odrl fldgi i|yd b;sß fõ'

    Step / mshjr 1:
    Calculate the total number of addresses
    iïmQ¾K ,smsk .Kk .Kkh lrkak
    The total number of IP addresses in the subnet can be calculated as 25 = 32
    Wmcd,fha we;s uq¿ IP ,smsk .Kk 25 = 32 f,i .Kkh l< yel

    Step / mshjr 2:
    Reserve addresses for network and broadcast
    cd,h iy úldYkh i|yd ,smsk fjkalr .kak
    In any subnet, two IP addresses are reserved,
    ´kEu Wmcd,hl" IP ,smsk folla fjka lr we;"
    The network address, which represents the subnet itself (in this case, 192.168.100.0). The broadcast
    address, which is used to communicate with all devices in the subnet (in this case, 192.168.100.31). These
    two addresses cannot be assigned to hosts.
    Wmcd,hu ksfhdackh lrk cd, ,smskh ^fuu wjia:dfõ§" 192.168.100.0& Wmcd,fha ish¨u Wmdx.
    iuÕ ikaksfõokh lsÍug Ndú;d lrk úldYk ,smskh ^fuu wjia:dfõ§" 192.168.100.31& fuu ,smsk
    fol Odrlhkag mejßh fkdyel'

    Step / mshjr 3:
    Calculate usable host addresses
    Ndú;d l< yels Odrl ,smsk .Kkh lrkak
    The number of usable host addresses is the total number of addresses minus the reserved addresses which
    is 32−2 = 30. These 30 usable IP addresses can be assigned to devices such as computers, printers, or other
    networked devices within the subnet.
    Ndú; l< yels Odrl ,smsk ixLHdj 32−2 = 30 jk iïmq¾K ,smsk .Kkska wvq lrk ,o fjka lr
    we;s ,smsk .Kkhs' fuu Ndú; l< yels IP ,smsk 30 mß.Kl" uqøK hka;%" fyda Wmcd,h ;=< we;s
    fjk;a cd,.; Wmdx. jeks Wmdx.j,g mejßh yel'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 2).
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 2) fõ'

 23. Which of the following are properties of the Transmission Control Protocol (TCP)?
     iïfm%aIK md,k kshudj,sfha (TCP) .=Kdx. my; tAjdfhka ljrlao @
    A - Detection and correction of any errors in a communication
        ikaksfõokhl fodaI we;akï tAjd y÷kd.ekSu yd ksjerÈ lsÍu
    B - Receiver acknowledging to the sender about the receipt of a data packet
        o;a; melÜgqjla ,enqKq nj ,nkakd (receiver) tjkakdg (sender) oekaùu
    C - Ensuring data packets are received in order
        o;a; melÜgq ms<sfj<ska ,efnk njg iy;sl ùu

     1) A only.          2) A and B only.    3) A and C only.     4) B and C only.     5) All of the above.
        A muKs.             A yd B muKs.        A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.         by; ish,a,u.

    Answer : 4) or 5)

    Explanation of each statement / tla tla m%ldYh meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    Statement / m%ldYh A
    Error Detection: TCP uses checksums for error detection. Each TCP segment includes a checksum field,
    which helps identify corrupted data during transmission. If a checksum mismatch occurs, TCP discards
    the segment and requests retransmission.
    fodaI y÷kd.ekSu( TCP fodaI y÷kd.ekSu i|yd fplaiï Ndú;d lrhs' iEu TCP fldgilgu checksum
    lafIa;%hla we;=<;a jk w;r th iïfm%aIKh w;r;=r ¥Is; o;a; y÷kd .ekSug WmldÍ fõ' fplaiï
    fkd.e,mSula isÿjqjfyd;a" TCP tu fldgi bj; oud kej; iïfm%aIKh b,a,d isà'

    Error Correction: While TCP does not correct errors in the traditional sense, it corrects communication
    issues through its retransmission mechanism. If an error is detected (ex: a packet is missing or
    corrupted), TCP ensures that the packet is resent until it is received correctly. So according to this
    argument A is correct.
    fodaI ksjerÈ lsÍu( TCP iïm%odhsl w¾:fhka fodaI ksjerÈ fkdlrk w;r" th tys kej; iïfm%aIK
    hdka;%Kh yryd ikaksfõok .eg¿ ksjerÈ lrhs' fodaIhla wkdjrKh jqjfyd;a ^Wod( melÜgqjla
    w;=reoyka ù fyda ¥Is; ù we;&" TCP th ksjerÈj ,efnk f;la melÜgqj kej; hjk nj iy;sl
    lrhs' tneúka fuu ;¾lhg wkqj A ksjerÈhs'

    Or

    While TCP does have error detection mechanisms, it doesn't have the capability to correct errors directly.
    If an error is detected, TCP retransmits the lost or corrupted packet. According to this argument A is
    incorrect.
    TCP i;=j fodaI y÷kd.ekSfï hdka;%Khka we;s w;r" thg iDcqju fodaI ksjerÈ lsÍfï yelshdjla
    fkdue;' fodaIhla wkdjrKh jqjfyd;a" ke;sjQ fyda ¥Is; melÜgqj TCP kej; iïfm%aIKh lrhs' fuu
    ;¾lhg wkqj A jerÈhs'

    Statement / m%ldYh B
    This is a fundamental property of TCP. It uses a mechanism called acknowledgements (ACKs) to confirm
    the successful receipt of data packets. This ensures reliable delivery. Therefore this statement is Correct.
    fuh TCP ys uQ,sl foam,ls' o;a; melÜ id¾:l f,i ,eîu ;yjqre lsÍu i|yd th acknowledgements
    (ACKs) kï hdka;%Khla Ndú;d lrhs' fuh úYajdiodhl fnod yeÍu iy;sl lrhs' tneúka fuu m%ldYh
    ksjer§hs'

    Statement / m%ldYh C
    TCP guarantees the ordered delivery of data packets. It numbers each packet and reassembles them in the
    correct order at the receiver's end. Therefore this statement is Correct.
    weKjqï l< o;a; melÜ fnod yeÍu TCP iy;sl lrhs' th iEu melÜgqjlau wxlkh lr tAjd
    .%dylhdf.a fl<jfra ksjerÈ wkqms<sfj,g kej; tl,ia lrhs' tneúka fuu m%ldYh ksjer§hs'

    Therefore, the correct answer can be 4) or 5) based on the above two arguments.
    tneúka" by; ;¾l fol u; mokïj ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 4) fyda 5) fõ'

 24. A new system must be delivered completely to the client by a given date. There should not be any partial
     deliveries. Further, the system architecture and design must be fully defined before any coding began.
     kj moaO;shla ksYaÑ; Èkhl § fiajd,dNshdg uqod yeßh hq;=h' w¾O uqod yeÍï (partial deliveries)
     fkd;sìh hq;=h' ;jo fla;kh (coding) weröug fmr moaO;s ks¾ñ;h (system architecture) yd ie,iqu
     iïmQ¾Kfhka kshu lr ;sìh hq;= h'
     Which of the following are suitable models to develop this system?
     fuu moaO;s ixj¾Okhg iqÿiq wdlD;s my; tAjdfhka fudkjdo@
     A - waterfall / Èhwe,s           B - spiral / i¾ms,                C - agile / iqp,H

     1) A only.           2) A and B only.    3) A and C only.     4) B and C only.     5) All of the above.
        A muKs.              A yd B muKs.        A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.         by; ish,a,u.

     Answer : 1 )

     Explanation of each option / tla tla úl,amh meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

     Option / úl,amh A
     This is suitable because the Waterfall model ensures Full requirements analysis and system design before
     coding begins. It is a sequential process, leading to a single, complete delivery. This makes it ideal for
     projects with fixed timelines and well-defined requirements.
     fuh iqÿiqhs' Èhwe,s wdlD;sh fla;SlrKh wdrïN lsÍug fmr iïmQ¾K wjYH;d úYaf,aIKh iy
     moaO;s ie,iqu iy;sl lrhs' th wkql%ñl l%shdj,shla jk w;r" th ;ks" iïmQ¾K fnod yeÍulg
     u. mdohs' fuh ia:djr ld, kshuhka iy fyd¢ka w¾: olajd we;s wjYH;d iys; jHdmD;s i|yd
     jvd;a iqÿiq fõ'

     Option / úl,amh B
     This is not suitable because the Spiral model follows iterative cycles, refining the system through
     prototypes. While it supports risk management, it does not prioritize a single, complete delivery and allows
     flexibility for partial deliveries.
     fuh iqÿiq fkdfõ" ukao i¾ms,dldr wdlD;sh uQ,dlD;s yryd moaO;sh msßmyÿ lrñka mqkrdj¾;k pl%
     wkq.ukh lrhs' th wjodkï l<ukdlrKhg iydh fok w;ru" th ;ks" iïmQ¾K fnodyeÍulg
     m%uqL;ajh fkdfok w;r w¾O fnodyeÍï i|yd kuHYS,S ùug bv i,ihs'

     Option / úl,amh C
     This is not suitable because Agile focuses on delivering small, incremental portions of the system. It is
     iterative and does not mandate a fully defined design upfront. Agile thrives in projects with evolving
     requirements but contradicts the specified need for a single, complete delivery.
     moaO;sfha l=vd" j¾Ol fldgia fnod yeÍu flfrys iqp,H wjOdkh fhduq lrk ksid fuh iqÿiq fkdfõ'
     th mqkrdj¾;kh jk w;r iïmQ¾Kfhka ks¾jpkh lrk ,o ie,iqula fmrg kshu fkdlrhs'
     úldYkh jk wjYH;d iys; jHdmD;sj, iqp,H iuDoaêu;a kuq;a ;ks" iïmQ¾K fnodyeÍula i|yd
     ksYaÑ; wjYH;djhg mgyeks fõ'

     Therefore, the correct answer is 1).
     tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 1) fõ'

     Extra Information / wu;r f;dr;=re(

     •   Iterative Model / mqkrdj¾;k wdlD;sh
         This develops a system in smaller iterations, refining the system with each cycle based on feedback
         and evolving requirements.
         fuh l=vd mqkrdj¾;k j,ska moaO;shla ixj¾Okh lrhs" m%;sfmdaIK iy mßKduh jk wjYH;d
         u; mokïj tla tla pl%h iuÕ moaO;sh msßmyÿ lrhs'

     •   Prototype Model / uQ,dlD;s wdlD;sh
         This creates a working prototype of the system to gather user feedback and refine requirements before
         the actual system is developed.
         fuh ienE moaO;sh ixj¾Okh lsÍug fmr mßYS,l m%;sfmdaIK /ia lsÍug iy wjYH;d msßmyÿ
         lsÍug moaO;sfha l%shdldÍ uQ,dlD;shla ks¾udKh lrhs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -29-                                         #ictfromabc


     •   RAD (Rapid Application Development / fõ.j;a fhÿï ixj¾Okh)
         Prioritizes rapid prototyping and quick feedback to speed up development. Focuses on user
         involvement. This is ideal for projects with tight deadlines and when quick delivery is needed.
         ixj¾Okh fõ.j;a lsÍu i|yd fõ.j;a uQ,dlD;slrKh iy blauka m%;sfmdaIK i|yd m%uqL;ajh
         fohs' mßYS,l iyNd.S;ajh flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrhs' fuh oeä ld,iSudjka iys; jHdmD;s
         i|yd iy blauka Ndr§u wjYH jQ úg jvd;a iqÿiq fõ'

 25. During the feasibility analysis of a software development project, it was discovered that the development
     team does not have the knowledge and the skills to develop the software. Which component of the
     feasibility study would have identified this issue?
     uDÿldx. ixj¾Ok jHdmD;shl YlH;d úYaf,aIKfha§ yÿkd.ekqfka uDÿldx.h ixj¾Okh lsÍug
     ixj¾Ok lKavdhug oekqu iy l=i,;d fkdue;s njh' YlH;d wOHhkfha l=uk ixrplh tu lreK
     y÷kd.kakg we;s o @
     1) economic feasibility / wd¾Ól YlH;dj                   2) legal feasibility / kS;suh YlH;dj
     3) operational feasibility / fufyhqï YlH;dj              4) schedule feasibility / ld, YlH;dj
     5) technical feasibility / ;dlaIKSl YlH;dj

     Answer : 5)

     Explanation of each option / tla tla úl,amh meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

     Option / úl,am 1)
     Not applicable here because the issue is about technical skills, not financial viability. Focuses on the
     financial aspects of the project, including cost-benefit analysis and return on investment.
     m%Yakh ;dlaIKSl l=i,;d ñi uQ,H YlH;djla fkdjk ksid fuh wod< fkdfõ' msßjeh-m%;s,dN
     úYaf,aIKh iy wdfhdackfha m%;s,dN we;=¿j jHdmD;sfha uQ,H wxY flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrhs'

     Option / úl,am 2)
     Not applicable here because the issue does not involve legal concerns. Ensures compliance with laws,
     regulations, and contractual obligations.
     .eg¨j kS;suh .eg¿ iïnkaO fkdjk ksid fuh wod< fkdfõ' kS;s" fr.=,dis iy .súiqï ne£ï j,g
     wkql+, ùu iy;sl lrhs'

     Option / úl,am 3)
     Not applicable here because the issue is about development skills, not system usability or alignment with
     operational goals. Evaluates whether the proposed system will meet organizational goals and user needs.
     m%Yakh jkafka ixj¾Ok l=i,;d ñi moaO;sfha Wmfhda.S;dj fyda fufyhqï b,lal iuÕ fm<.eiaùu
     fkdjk ksid fuh wod< fkdfõ' fhdað; moaO;sh wdh;ksl b,lal iy mßYS,l wjYH;d imqrd,kafkao
     hkak we.hSug ,la lrhs'

     Option / úl,am 4)
     Not applicable here because the issue is unrelated to project timelines. Assesses whether the project can
     be completed within the specified timeframe.
     .eg¨j jHdmD;s ld, kshuhkag iïnkaO ke;s ksid fuh wod< fkdfõ' kshñ; ld, iSudj ;=< jHdmD;sh
     wjika l< yelso hkak ;lafiare lrhs'

     Option / úl,am 5)
     In this case, the lack of knowledge and skills within the development team is a technical limitation. The
     technical feasibility study would have assessed the team’s capabilities and highlighted any gaps,
     recommending actions such as Providing additional training to the team, Hiring skilled developers or
     consultants, Revisiting the project scope to align it with available technical resources.

 26. Which of the following shows the correct order of stages in the System Development Life Cycle?
     moaO;s ixj¾Ok Ôjk pl%fha (System Development Life Cycle) wÈhr ksjerÈj fm<.iajd we;af;a
     my; ljrl o @
    1) Feasibility study →Requirements analysis→System design →Implementation →Testing →Deployment
       YlH;d wOHhkh→wjYH;d úYaf,aIKh→moaO;s ks¾udKh→l%shd;aul lsÍu→mÍlaIdj→fhoùu
    2) Feasibility study→System design→Requirements analysis→Implementation→Testing→Deployment
       YlH;d wOHhkh→moaO;s ks¾udKh→wjYH;d úYaf,aIKh→l%shd;aul lsÍu→mÍlaIdj→fhoùu
    3) Requirements analysis→Feasibility study→System design→Testing→Deployment→Implementation
       wjYH;d úYaf,aIKh→YlH;d wOHhkh→moaO;s ks¾udKh→mÍlaIdj→fhoùu→l%shd;aul lsÍu
    4) Requirements analysis→System design→Feasibility study→Deployment→Testing→Implementation
       wjYH;d úYaf,aIKh→moaO;s ks¾udKh→YlH;d wOHhkh" fhoùu" mÍlaIdj" l%shd;aul lsÍu
    5) System design→Requirements analysis→Feasibility study→Implementation→Testing→Deployment
       moaO;s ks¾udKh→wjYH;d úYaf,aIKh→YlH;d wOHhkh→l%shd;aul lsÍu→mÍlaIdj→fhoùu

    Answer : 1)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    1   Feasibility Study / YlH;d wOHhkh
        This is the initial stage where the viability of the project is assessed. The feasibility study looks at
        whether the project is technically, economically, and operationally possible. Before proceeding with
        any detailed design or analysis, it’s important to ensure the project can actually be completed given
        the available resources and constraints.
        jHdmD;sfha YlH;dj ;lafiare lrk wdrïNl wÈhr fuhhs' YlH;d wOHhkh u.ska jHdmD;sh
        ;dlaIKSl jYfhka" wd¾Ól jYfhka iy l%shd;aul úh yels o hkak fidhd n,hs' ´kEu
        iúia;rd;aul ie,iqula fyda úYaf,aIKhla bÈßhg f.k hdug fmr" mj;sk iïm;a iy iSudjka
        ,nd § jHdmD;sh ienúkau ksu l< yels nj iy;sl lsÍu jeo.;a fõ'

    2   Requirements Analysis / wjYH;d úYaf,aIKh
        In this stage, detailed requirements of the system are gathered from the stakeholders, including users
        and clients. The goal is to understand exactly what the system must do.
        fuu wÈhf¾§" mßYS,lhska iy fiajdodhlhska we;=¿ md¾Yjlrejkaf.ka moaO;sfha iúia;rd;aul
        wjYH;d tla/ia lrkq ,efí' b,lalh jkafka moaO;sh l< hq;= foa ksjerÈj f;areï .ekSuhs'

    3   System Design / moaO;s ks¾udKh
        Based on the requirements gathered, the system architecture and design are created. This stage defines
        the system's structure and components, including hardware, software, and data models. The system
        can’t be designed without a clear understanding of what it needs to do, so this comes after requirements
        gathering.
        tl;= lrk ,o wjYH;d u; mokïj" moaO;s ks¾ñ;h iy ie,iqu ks¾udKh lr we;' fuu
        wÈhr oDVdx." uDÿldx. iy o;a; wdlD;s we;=¿j moaO;sfha jHqyh iy ixrpl ks¾jpkh lrhs'
        th l< hq;= foa ms<sn| meyeÈ,s wjfndaOhlska f;drj moaO;sh ie,iqï l< fkdyel" tneúka fuh
        wjYH;d tl;= lsÍfuka miqj meñfKa'

    4   Implementation (or Coding) / l%shd;aul lsÍu ^fyda fla;SlrKh&
        This is the actual development stage where the system is built according to the design specifications.
        Once the system design is finalized, the system is implemented by writing the code and setting up the
        necessary environment.
        ie,iqï msßú;rhkag wkqj moaO;sh f.dvk.d we;s ienE ixj¾Ok wÈhr fuhhs' moaO;s ie,iqu
        wjika jQ miq" fla;h ,sùfuka iy wjYH mßirh ieliSfuka moaO;sh l%shd;aul fõ'

    5   Testing / mÍlaIdj
        In this phase, the system is tested to ensure that it works as expected. This includes functional,
        performance, and security testing to identify bugs and issues.
        fuu wÈhf¾§" th wfmalaIs; mßÈ l%shd;aul jk nj iy;sl lsÍu i|yd moaO;sh mÍlaId lrkq
        ,efí' fodaI iy .eg¿ y÷kd .ekSu i|yd l%shdldÍ" ld¾h idOkh iy wdrlaIl mÍlaIKh fuhg
        we;=<;a fõ'

    6   Deployment / fhoùu
        This is the final stage, where the system is delivered to the users and becomes operational. It may
        involve user training and final adjustments. The system is deployed only after it has been tested and
        is deemed ready for use.
        moaO;sh mßYS,lhka fj; ,nd § l%shd;aul jk wjidk wÈhr fuhhs' thg mßYS,l mqyqKqj iy

 27. Which of the following is not true regarding the prototyping technique used in system development?
     moaO;s ixj¾Okfha§ Ndú;d jk uq,dlD;slrKh (prototyping) iïnkaOfhka my; ljrla wi;H o@
    1) Prototypes need to be approved by the users, during the ‘system testing’ phase.
       moaO;s mÍlaId (system testing) wÈhf¾§ uQ,dlD;s" mßYS,lhkaf.ka wkqu; úh hq;=h'
    2) Prototyping is neither necessary nor appropriate in every project.
       uQ,dlD;slrKh iEu jHdmD;shlgu wjYH fyda iqÿiq fyda fkdfõ'
    3) Successful prototyping helps to develop a system that better addresses user needs and expectations.
       id¾:l uQ,dlD;slrKh mßYS,l wjYH;d iy n,dfmdfrd;a;= fyd¢ka imqrd,k moaO;s ixj¾Okh
       i|yd bjy,a fõ'
    4) Successful prototyping helps to eliminate costly late changes to a system.
       moaO;shlg úhoï iys; miq fjkialï lsÍu ;=rka lsÍug id¾:l uQ,dlD;slrKh bjy,a fõ'
    5) To get the benefits of prototyping, user feedback on the prototypes is extremely important.
       uq,dlD;slrKfha jdis ,nd.ekSug uq,dlD;s ms<sn| mßYS,l m%;spdr w;sYhska jeo.;a fõ

    Answer : 1)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    Prototypes are primarily used in the early stages of system development to gather user feedback and refine
    requirements. The statement "Prototypes need to be approved by the users during the 'system testing'
    phase" is incorrect because prototyping focuses on creating initial models for user interaction before full
    development begins. System testing occurs much later in the development lifecycle, after the system is
    fully implemented, and is meant to validate the complete product rather than prototypes.
    mßYS,l m%;sfmdaIK tla/ia lsÍu iy wjYH;d msßmyÿ lsÍu i|yd uQ,dlD;s uQ,sl jYfhka moaO;s
    ixj¾Okfha uq,a wjêfha§ Ndú;d fõ' "moaO;s mÍlaId lsÍfï wÈhf¾ § mßYS,lhska úiska uQ,dlD;slKh
    wkqu; l< hq;=h" hk m%ldYh jerÈh" ukao uQ,dlD;slrKh mQ¾K ixj¾Okh wdrïN ùug fmr
    mßYS,l wka;¾l%shd i|yd uQ,sl wdlD;s ks¾udKh lsÍu flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrk neúks' moaO;sh
    iïmQ¾Kfhka l%shd;aul lsÍfuka miqj" ixj¾Ok Ôjk pl%fha fndfyda miqld,Skj moaO;s mÍlaIdj isÿ
    jk w;r" uQ,dlD;slrK j,g jvd iïmQ¾K ksIamdokh j,x.= lsÍug woyia flf¾'

    Therefore, linking prototype approval to the system testing phase is inaccurate, making Option 1) the
    correct answer.
    tneúka" moaO;s mÍlaIK wÈhr fj; uQ,dlD;s wkque;sh iïnkaO lsÍu jerÈhs" úl,am 1) ksjerÈ
    ms<s;=r njg m;a lrhs'

    The other options are valid statements / wfkl=;a úl,am j,x.= m%ldYhka fõ'

    •   Option 2) is true because prototyping is not suitable for every project, as some may rely on alternative
        methodologies like waterfall.
        úl,am 2) i;H jkafka uQ,dlD;slrKh iEu jHdmD;shla i|ydu iqÿiq fkdjk ksid" iuyre Èhwe,s
        wdlD;sh jeks úl,am l%ufõo u; úYajdih ;eìh yels neúks'
    •   Option 3) accurately describes how prototyping helps design systems that meet user expectations
        through iterative feedback.
        úl,am 3) mqkrdj¾;k m%;sfmdaIK yryd mßYS,l wfmalaIdjka imqrd,k moaO;s ie,iqï lsÍug
        uQ,dlD;slrKh Wmldr jk wdldrh ksjerÈj úia;r lrhs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -32-                                          #ictfromabc


    •   Option 4) highlights how successful prototyping reduces late-stage changes by identifying issues early.
        4) jeks úl,am uÕska id¾:l uQ,dlD;slrKh uÕska .eg¿ l,ska y÷kd .ekSfuka m%udo-wÈhr
        fjkialï wvq lrk wdldrh biau;= lrhs'

    •   Option 5) emphasizes the importance of user feedback in improving prototypes.
        úl,am 5) uÕska uQ,dlD;s jeäÈhqKq lsÍfï§ mßYS,l m%;sfmdaIKj, jeo.;alu wjOdrKh lrhs'

    So, these options correctly describe aspects of prototyping, leaving Option 1) as the only incorrect
    statement.

 28. Which of the following statements regarding software tests are correct?
     uDÿldx. mÍlaId (software tests) iïnkaOfhka my; ljr j.ka;s ksjerÈ fõo@
    A - White-box rests involve testing the internal structures and workings of a software.
        Yafõ; uxcqid (White-box) mÍlaIdjkag" uDÿldx.hl wNHka;r jHqyhka yd l%shdlsÍfï úê
        msßlaiSu wh;a fõ'
    B - Unit tests are usually performed after the system test.
        idudkHfhka moaO;s mÍlaIdfjka miqj tAll mÍlaIdjka isÿ flf¾'
    C - System developers should take every effort to make the acceptance test suecessful.

    1) A only.           2) A and B only.    3) A and C only.     4) B and C only.    5) All of the above.
       A muKs.              A yd B muKs.        A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.        by; ish,a,u.

    Answer: 3)

    Explanation of each statement / tla tla m%ldYh meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    Statement / m%ldYh A
    White-box testing (also known as structural or glass-box testing) involves testing the internal structures,
    logic, and implementation of the software. Testers must have knowledge of the code and its internal
    workings.
    Yafõ; uxcqid mÍlaIdj ^jHqyd;aul fyda ùÿre fmÜá mÍlaIdj f,io ye¢kafõ& uDÿldx.fha wNHka;r
    jHqyhka" ;¾lkh iy l%shd;aul lsÍu mÍlaId lsÍu we;=<;a fõ' mÍlaIlhskag fla;h iy tys
    wNHka;r l%shdldß;ajh ms<sn| oekqula ;sìh hq;=h'

    This statement is true.
    fuu m%ldYh i;Hhls'

    Statement / m%ldYh B
    Unit testing is the process of testing individual components or modules of a program in isolation. It is
    usually performed at the earliest stages of testing, before integration testing and system testing.
    tAll mÍlaIdj hkq jevigykl ;ks ixrpl fyda fudähq, yqol,dj mÍlaId lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs' th
    idudkHfhka isÿ lrkq ,nkafka mÍlaIKj, uq,a wjêfha§" tAldnoaO;d mÍlaIdjg iy moaO;s mÍlaIdjg
    fmrh'

    System testing, on the other hand, is a high-level test performed after the entire system is integrated to
    ensure it meets the requirements.
    wfkla w;g" moaO;s mÍlaIdj hkq wjYH;d imqrd,k nj iy;sl lsÍu i|yd iïmQ¾K moaO;shu
    tAldnoaO lsÍfuka miqj isÿ lrkq ,nk by< uÜgfï mÍlaIKhls'

    Therefore, unit tests are performed before system tests, not after them.
    tneúka" moaO;s mÍlaIK j,g fmr tAll mÍlaIK isÿ lrkq ,efí" tAjdg miqj fkdfõ'

    This statement is false.
    fuu m%ldYh wi;Hhls'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                             -33-                                         #ictfromabc


    Statement / m%ldYh C
    Acceptance testing is conducted to determine whether the system meets the business requirements and is
    ready for deployment. It is usually performed by the client or end-users.
    moaO;sh jHdmdßl wjYH;d imqrd,kafkao iy fhoùug iQodkïo hkak ;SrKh lsÍu i|yd ms<s.ekSfï
    mÍlaIKh mj;ajkq ,efí' th idudkHfhka isÿ lrkq ,nkafka fiajdodhlhd fyda wjidk mßYS,lhka
    úisks'

    While system developers should ensure the software meets the client’s requirements, their primary
    responsibility is to develop a system that adheres to specifications. They should avoid manipulating the
    testing process to ensure artificial success.
    moaO;s ixj¾Olhska úiska uDÿldx.h fiajdodhlhdf.a wjYH;d imqrd,k njg iy;sl úh hq;= w;r"
    Tjqkaf.a uQ,sl j.lSu jkafka msßú;rhkag wkq.; jk moaO;shla ixj¾Okh lsÍuhs' lD;su id¾:l;ajh

    This statement is controversial and can be interpreted differently:

        moaO;sfha .=Kd;aulNdjh iy ksjerÈ nj iy;sl lsÍu f,i w¾:l:kh lrkafka kï" th i;H
        f,i ie,lsh yelsh'

 31. Consider the following relation about a student who is registered in a programme at an institute:
     wdh;khl jevigyklg ,shdmÈxÑ ù we;s isiqfjl=g wod< my; iïnkaOh i,lkak'

     STUDENT(Sno, Snic, Sname, Sphone, Prog_number)

     Note:

     Sno              -       the unique registration number of the student
                              isiqjdf.a wkkH ,shdmÈxÑ wxlh
     Snic             -       the national identity card number of the student
                              isiqjdf.a cd;sl ye÷kqïm;a wxlh
     Sname            -       the name of the student
                              isiqjdf.a ku

     Sphone           -       a phone number of the student
                              isiqjdf.a ÿrl:k wxlh

     Prog_ number -           the unique number of the programme for which the student has registered
                              isiqjd ,shdmÈxÑ ù we;s jevigyfka wkkH wxlh
     Which of the following are correct?
     my; ljrla ksjerÈ o@
     A - Sno can be the primary key. / Sno m%d:ñl (primary) h;=rla úh yels h'
     B - Snic can be a candidate key. / Snic ksrEmH (candidate) h;=rla úh yels h'
     C - Prog_number can be a foreign key. / Prog_number wd.ka;=l (foreign) h;=rla úh yels h'

     1) A only.           2) A and B only.     3) A and C only.     4) B and C only.     5) All of the above.
        A muKs.              A yd B muKs.         A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.         by; ish,a,u.

     Answer : 5)

     Explanation of each statement / tla tla m%ldYh meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

     Statement / m%ldYh A
     A primary key must satisfy these two conditions.
     m%d:ñl h;=rla fuu fldkafoais fol imqrd,sh hq;=h

     1. It must uniquely identify each record (no duplicates).
        th tla tla jd¾;dj wkkH f,i y÷kd.; hq;=h ^wkqmsgm;a ke;&'

     2. It must not be null (every record must have a value for the primary key).
        th Y=kH fkdúh hq;=h ^iEu jd¾;djlu m%d:ñl h;=r i|yd w.hla ;sìh hq;=h&'

     In this relation, the attribute Sno is described as a unique registration number for each student. This means:
     fuu iïnkaOh ;=<" Sno Wm,laIKh iEu isiqfjl=gu wkkH ,shdmÈxÑ wxlhla f,i úia;r flf¾'
     fuhska woyia jkafka(

     -     Every student has a distinct Sno, so there are no duplicates.
           iEu isiqfjl=gu fjkia Sno we;" tneúka wkqmsgm;a fkdue;'

     -     It is reasonable to assume that Sno cannot be null because every student must have a registration
           number. / iEu isiqfjl=gu ,shdmÈxÑ wxlhla ;sìh hq;= neúka Sno Y=kH úh fkdyels hehs
           Wml,amkh lsÍu idOdrK h'

     So, Sno qualifies as a primary key.
     fï wkqj" Sno m%d:ñl h;=rla f,i iqÿiqlï ,nhs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -38-                                           #ictfromabc


    Statement / m%ldYh B
    A candidate key is an attribute (or a combination of attributes) that can uniquely identify each record in
    the table, but it is not chosen as the primary key.
    wfmalaIl h;=rla hkq j.=fõ we;s iEu jd¾;djlau wkkHj y÷kd.; yels Wm,laIKhla ^fyda
    Wm,laIKj, tl;=jls&" kuq;a th m%d:ñl h;=r f,i f;dardf.k fkdue;'

    The attribute Snic (National ID Card number) is unique for every individual because it is assigned by a
    government or relevant authority.
    Snic ^cd;sl ye÷kqïm;a wxlh& hk Wm,laIKh rchla fyda wod< wêldßhla úiska mjrkq ,nk
    neúka iEu mqoa.,fhl=gu wkkH fõ'

    -   It is reasonable to assume that no 2 students will have the same Snic, and every student will have one.
        lsisÿ isiqka fofofkl=g tlu Snic fkdue;s nj;a" iEu isiqfjl=gu tlla we;s nj;a Wml,amkh
        lsÍu idOdrK h'

    -   Since Snic satisfies the conditions for uniqueness and non-nullability, it can be a candidate key.
        Snic iqúfYaI;ajh iy Y=kH fkdjk nj i|yd fldkafoais ;Dma;su;a lrk neúka" th wfmalaIl
        h;=rla úh yel'

    (Note: A table can have multiple candidate keys, but only one of them is chosen as the primary key. For
    example, Sno could be the primary key, while Snic remains a candidate key.)
    (igyk( j.=jlg wfmalaIl h;=re lsysmhla ;sìh yel" kuq;a tAjdhska tlla muKla m%d:ñl h;=r f,i
    f;dard we;' WodyrKhla f,i" Sno m%d:ñl h;=r úh yels w;r" Snic wfmalaIl h;=rla f,i mj;S')

    Statement / m%ldYh C
    A foreign key is an attribute in one table that refers to the primary key in another table. It establishes a
    relationship between two tables.
    wd.ka;=l h;=rla hkq tla j.=jl we;s Wm,laIKhla jk w;r th ;j;a j.=jl we;s m%d:ñl h;=rg
    fhduq lrhs' th j.= folla w;r iïnkaO;djhla we;s lrhs'

    The attribute Prog_number represents the program for which the student is registered.
    Prog_number Wm,laIKh YsIHhd ,shdmÈxÑ lr we;s jevigyk ksfhdackh lrhs'

    -   It is described as a unique number for the program, implying it likely matches the primary key of a
        PROGRAMME table (ex: PROGRAMME(Prog_number, Prog_name, Duration)).
        th jevigyk i|yd wkkH wxlhla f,i úia;r lr we;s w;r" thska weÕfjkafka th
        PROGRAMME j.=jl (Wod( PROGRAMME(Prog_number, Prog_name, Duration)) m%d:ñl h;=r
        iuÕ .e<fmk njhs'

    -   So, Prog_number in the STUDENT table can serve as a foreign key referencing the Prog_number
        in the PROGRAMME table.
        fï wkqj" STUDENT j.=fõ Prog_number, PROGRAMME j.=fõ Prog_number fhduq lrk
        wd.ka;=l h;=rla f,i lghq;= l< yel'

    So, all statements (A, B and C) are correct.
    fï wkqj" ish¨u m%ldYhka (A, B iy C) ksjerÈ fõ'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 5).

 32. Which of the following are true?
     my; ljrla ksjerÈ o@
     A - A table can have multiple candidate keys.
         j.=jlg fndfyda ksrEmH h;=re (candidate keys) ;sìh yels h'
     B - A primary key is always a candidate key.
         m%d:ñl h;=r (primary key) iEuúgu ksrEmH h;=rls'
     C - A candidate key of one table can be used as a foreign key in another table.
         tla j.=jl ksrEmH h;=r" fjk;a j.=jl wd.ka;=l h;=rla (foreign key) f,i Ndú;d l< yelsh'

     1) A only.           2) A and B only.      3) A and C only.     4) B and C only.     5) All of the above.
        A muKs.              A yd B muKs.          A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.         by; ish,a,u.

     Answer : 5)
     Explanation of each statement / tla tla m%ldYh meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

     Statement / m%ldYh A
     A candidate key is an attribute (or a set of attributes) that can uniquely identify a record in a table.
     wfmalaIl h;=rla hkq j.=jl we;s jd¾;djla wkkHj y÷kd.; yels Wm,laIKhla ^fyda Wm,laIK
     iuQyhls&'
     A table can have multiple candidate keys, but only one is selected as the primary key. For example:
     j.=jlg wfmalaIl h;=re lsysmhla ;sìh yels kuq;a m%d:ñl h;=r f,i f;dardf.k we;af;a tlla
     muKs' WodyrK jYfhka(
     In a table STUDENT(Sno, Snic, Sname), both Sno (Student Number) and Snic (National ID Card) can
     uniquely identify each record, so both are candidate keys.
     j.= STUDENT(Sno, Snic, Sname), Sno ^YsIH wxlh& iy Snic ^cd;sl ye÷kqïm;& hk folgu tla
     tla jd¾;dj wkkH f,i y÷kd.; yels neúka folu wfmalaIl h;=re fõ'
     This statement is true because having multiple candidate keys in a table is common.
     j.=jl nyq wfmalaIl h;=re ;sîu idudkH fohla neúka fuu m%ldYh i;H fõ'

     Statement / m%ldYh B
     A primary key is chosen from among the candidate keys of a table.
     j.=jl wfmalaIl h;=re w;ßka m%d:ñl h;=rla f;dard .kq ,efí'
     Since the primary key must satisfy the requirements of a candidate key (uniqueness and non-nullability),
     it is always a candidate key.
     m%d:ñl h;=r wfmalaIl h;=rl wjYH;d imqrd,sh hq;= neúka ^iqúfYaIS;ajh iy Y=kH fkdjk nj&"
     th ieuúgu wfmalaIl h;=rls'
     This statement is true.
     fuu m%ldYh i;H fõ'

     Statement / m%ldYh C
     A foreign key is an attribute in 1 table that references the primary key (or a candidate key) in another table.
     wd.ka;=l h;=rla hkq tla j.=jl we;s Wm,laIKhla jk w;r th ;j;a j.=jl m%d:ñl h;=r ^fyda
     wfmalaIl h;=rla& fõ'
     In a STUDENT table, Prog_number could be a candidate key for the PROGRAMME table and also serve
     as a foreign key in STUDENT to link the program details.
     STUDENT j.=jl" Prog_number PROGRAMME j.=j i|yd wfmalaIl h;=rla úh yels w;r
     jevigyka úia;r iïnkaO lsÍug STUDENT ys wd.ka;=l h;=rla f,i o l%shd lrhs'
     This statement is true because any candidate key, including the primary key, can act as a foreign key in
     another table.
     fuu m%ldYh i;H jkafka m%d:ñl h;=r we;=¿j ´kEu wfmalaIl h;=rla fjk;a j.=jl wd.ka;=l
     h;=rla f,i l%shd l< yels neúks'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                              -40-                                          #ictfromabc


    Conclusion / ks.ukh(

    All statements (A, B and C) are correct.
    ish¨u m%ldYhka (A, B iy C) ksjerÈ fõ'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 5).
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 5) fõ'

 33. Which of the following are examples of one-to many relationships?
     tAl-nyq iïnkaO;dj,g my; ljrla WodyrK fõo@

    A- A customer can place many orders, but each order is placed by one customer.
       mdßfNda.slfhl=g weKjqï lsysmhla l< yels kï kuq;a tla weKjqulg we;af;a tla mdßfNda.slhl=
       muKs'
    B- An employee can be assigned to multiple projects, and each project can have multiple employees.
       tla fiajlfhl= jHdmD;s .Kkdjlg m;a l< yelsh' tla jHdmD;shlg fndfyda fiajlhka isáh yelsh'
    C- One department has one manager, and each manager manages multiple departments.
       tla fomd¾;fïka;=jlg isákafka tla l<ukdlrefjls' iEu l<ukdlrefjl=u fndfyda fomd¾;fïka;=
       l<ukdlrKh lrhs'
    D- A supplier can supply only one item, and an item can be supplied by only one supplier.
       iemhqïlrejl=g tla whs;uhla muKla iemhsh yelsh tla whs;uhla iemfhkafka tla iemhqïlrefjl=
       f.ka muKs'

    1) A and B only      2) A and C only       3) A and D only      4) B and C only       5) C and D only
       A iy B muKs          A iy C muKs           A iy D muKs          B iy C muKs           C iy D muKs

    Answer : 2)

    Types of relationships/ iïnkaO;d j¾.(

    •   One-to-one relationship / tAl-tAl iïnkaO;d
        Each record in Table A relates to exactly one record in Table B.
        A j.=fõ we;s iEu jd¾;djlau B j.=fõ tla jd¾;djlg iïnkaO fõ'
    •   One-to-many relationship / tAl-nyq iïnkaO;d
        One record in Table A can relate to many records in Table B, but each record in Table B is associated
        with only one record in Table A.
        A j.=fõ we;s tla jd¾;djla B j.=fõ fndfyda jd¾;d j,g iïnkaO úh yels kuq;a B j.=fõ iEu
        jd¾;djlau A j.=fõ tla jd¾;djla iuÕ muKla iïnkaO fõ'
    •   Many-to-many relationship / nyq-nyq iïnkaO;d
        Multiple records in Table A relate to multiple records in Table B. This is typically implemented using
        a junction table.
        A j.=fõ we;s nyq jd¾;d lsysmhla B j.=fõ jd¾;d lsysmhlg iïnkaO fõ' fuh idudkHfhka
        l%shd;aul jkafka ikaê j.=jla Ndú;d lrñks'

    Let’s go through each scenario given as A to D.
    A isg D olajd ,nd § we;s tla tla wjia:dj yryd huq

    Scenario/ wjia:dj A)
    Represents a typical one-to-many relationship, where one customer can place multiple orders, but each
    order is associated with only one customer.
    tla mdßfNda.slfhl=g nyq weKjqï l< yels" kuq;a iEu weKjqulau tla mdßfNda.slfhl= iuÕ muKla
    iïnkaO jk idudkH tAl-nyq in|;djla ksfhdackh lrhs'

    Scenario/ wjia:dj B)
    Describes a many-to-many relationship, as multiple employees can work on multiple projects.
    nyq fiajlhskag nyq jHdmD;sj, jev l< yels neúka" nyq-nyq in|;djla úia;r lrhs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                  -41-                                          #ictfromabc


    Scenario/ wjia:dj C)
    One manager manages multiple departments. Each department is managed by only one manager.
    Therefore this is a one-to-many relationship.
    tla l<ukdlrefjl= fomd¾;fïka;= lsysmhla l<ukdlrKh lrhs' iEu fomd¾;fïka;=jlau
    l<ukdlrKh lrkq ,nkafka tla l<ukdlrefjl= úisks' tneúka fuh tAl-nyq in|;djls'

    Scenario/ wjia:dj D)
    Describes a one-to-one relationship where a supplier provides only one item, and each item is supplied by
    only one supplier.
    iemhqïlrefjl= tla whs;uhla muKla imhk w;r" iEu whs;uhlau tla iemhqïlrefjl= úiska
    muKla imhkq ,nk tAl-tAl iïnkaO;djla úia;r lrhs'

    Both A and C are one-to-many relationships.
    A iy C hk folu tAl-nyq iïnkaO;d fõ'

    Therefore, the answer is 2).
    tneúka" ms<s;=r 2) fõ'


 34. Match the Normal forms labelled from 0 to 3 to the corresponding descriptions labelled from A to D.
     my; 0 isg 3 f;la f,an, lrwe;s m%u; wjia:d A isg D f;la f,an, lr we;s úia;r iuÕ .,mkak'

       Normal form/ m%u; wjia:dj                      Description/ úia;rh
      0- Zero normal form                       A- Single valued attribute
         Y=kH m%u; wjia:dj                         tAl w.h Wm,laIK
      1- First normal form                      B- Full functional dependency
         m%:u m%u; wjia:dj                         mQ¾K ld¾hnoaO mrdh;a;;dj
      2- Second normal form                     C- Repeating data
         fojk m%u; wjia:dj                         o;a; kej; ;sîu'
      3- Third normal form                      D- Transitive dependency
         f;jk m%u; wjia:dj                         ixl%dka;s mrdh;a;;dj

    1) 0 - A, 1 - B, 2 - C, 3 – D       2) 0 - A, 1 - C, 2 - B, 3 - D    3) 0 - B, 1 - C, 2 - A, 3 - D
    4) 0 - C, 1 - A, 2 - D, 3 – B       5) 0 - D, 1 - B, 2 - C, 3 - A

    Answer : 4)

    Database Normalization/ o;a; iuqodh m%u;lrKh
    Database normalization organizes data to reduce duplication and prevent errors during updates, deletions,
    or insertions. It ensures efficient storage and maintains data accuracy by structuring it into related tables.
    o;a; iuqodh m%u;lrKh uÕska o;a; wkqmsgm;a lsÍu wvq lsÍug iy hdj;ald,Sk lsÍï" uldoeóï
    fyda we;=<;a lsÍï j,§ fodaI je<elaùug o;a; ixúOdkh lrhs' th ld¾hlaIu .nvd lsÍu iy;sl
    lrk w;r wod< j.= j,g jHqy.; lsÍfuka o;a; ksrjoH;djh mj;ajd .kS'

          Normal form                                                                          Description
          m%u; wjia:dj                                                                          úia;rh
      0   Zero normal form         Data is stored without any structure or rules. Tables   C Repeating data
          Y=kH m%u;                contain no relationships or constraints.                  o;a; kej;
          wjia:dj                  lsisÿ jHqyhla fyda Í;shla fkdue;sj o;a; .nvd              ;sîu'
                                   lr we;' j.= j, iïnkaO;d fyda iSudjka fkdue;'
      1   First normal form        Eliminates duplicate groups by ensuring data is         A Single valued
          m%:u m%u;                atomic. Ensures that each column contains unique,         attribute
          wjia:dj                  indivisible values.                                       tAl w.h
                                   o;a; mrudKql nj iy;sl lsÍu u.ska wkqmsgm;a                Wm,laIK
                                   lKavdhï bj;a lrhs' iEu ;Srejlgu wkkH"
                                   fkdfnÈh yels w.hka wvx.= nj iy;sl lrhs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                 -42-                                         #ictfromabc



      2     Second normal          All non-key attributes are functionally dependent on   D Transitive
            form                   the entire primary key. Removes partial                  dependency
            fojk m%u;              dependencies on a composite key. Include transitive      ixl%dka;s
            wjia:dj                dependencies.                                            mrdh;a;;dj
                                   ish¨u h;=re-fkdjk Wm,laIK iïmQ¾K m%d:ñl

                              iy;sl lrhs'

 35. What is the primary purpose of database normalization?
     o;a; iuqod m%u;lrkfha uQ,sl wruqK l=ulao@
    1) Eliminating data redundancy and anomalies
       wkq msgm;a ùu iy fkd.e,mSï bj;a lsÍu'
    2) Increasing the number of tables in the database
       o;a; iuqodfha j.= .Kk jeälsÍu'
    3) Organizing data into logical structures and relations
       o;a; ;¾ldkql+, jHqyhkag yd iïnkaO;djhkag ms<sfh< lsÍu'
    4) Simplifying database queries
       o;a; iuqod úuiqï ir, lsÍu'
    5) Speeding up database queries
       o;a; iuqod úuiqï fõ.j;a lsÍu'

    Answer : 1 )

    Redundancy / wkq msgm;a ùu
    Redundancy occurs when the same piece of data is stored in multiple places. This leads to wastage of
    storage space and creates difficulties in maintaining data consistency.
    tlu o;a; fldgila' ia:dk lsysmhl .nvd lr we;s úg wkq msgm;a ùu we;sfõ' fuh .nvd bv
    kdia;shg ;=vq fok w;r o;a; wkql+,;dj mj;ajd .ekSfï ÿIalr;d we;s lrhs'

    Data Anomalies / o;a; fkd.e,mSï

    −     Insertion Anomaly / we;=<;a lsÍfï úIu;dj
          Inability to add data without including unnecessary or unrelated data.
          wkjYH fyda fkdne¢ o;a; we;=<;a fkdlr o;a; tla lsÍug fkdyels ùu'

    −     Update Anomaly / hdj;ald,Sk lsÍfï úIu;dj
          Updating data in one place but not in another, leading to inconsistencies.
          tla ia:dkhl o;a; hdj;ald,Sk lsÍu fkdj ;j;a ia:dkhl" fkd.e,mSï we;s lrhs'

    −     Deletion Anomaly / uldoeófï úIu;dj
          Unintended loss of data when other data is deleted.
          fjk;a o;a; uld oeuQ úg wkfmalaIs; o;a; ke;sùu'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -43-                                          #ictfromabc


    Let’s go through the given options / ,nd § we;s úl,am yryd huq'

    Option / úl,amh 1)
    The primary purpose of database normalization is to organize data efficiently by reducing redundancy
    (repeated data) and preventing anomalies during data operations. Redundancy and anomalies waste
    storage and complicates maintaining consistency.
    o;a; iuqodh m%u;lrKfha uQ,sl wruqK jkafka wkq msgm;a ^mqkrdj¾;k o;a;& wvq lsÍu iy o;a;
    fufyhqï j,§ úIu;d je<elaùu u.ska o;a; ld¾hlaIuj ixúOdkh lsÍuhs' wkq msgm;a ùu iy
    úIu;d .nvd bv kdia;s lrhs tfiau wkql+,;dj mj;ajd .ekSu ixlS¾K lrhs'

    Option / úl,amh 2)

    m%u;lrKh iuyr wjia:dj,§ ;j;a j.= j,g ;=vq Èh yels w;r" fuh tys b,lalh fkdj o;a;
    M,odhS f,i ixúOdkh lsÍfï w;=re M,hls'

    fuh m%u;lrKfha m%;s,dNhla jk kuq;a tys uQ,sl wruqK fkdfõ' ;d¾lsl ixúOdkh isÿjkafka wkq

    úuiqï ir, úh yel" kuq;a o;a; j.= lsysmhla yryd me;sÍ we;s neúka m%u;lrKh iuyr úg
