 36. Which of the following will exchange of ‘Mahawa’ in the ‘City’ attribute of USER in relation to ‘Maho’?
     my; ljrla u.ska USER iïnkaOfha ‘City’ Wm,laIKfha we;s ish¨ ‘Mahawa’ f,i we;s ;eka ‘Maho’
     f,i fjkia lrhs o@
     1)   MODIFY USER SET City = ‘Maho’ WHERE City = ‘Mahawa’;
     2)   MODIFY USER SET City = ‘Mahawa’ INTO City = ‘Maho’;
     3)   UPDATE USER SET City = ‘Mahawa’ INTO City = ‘Maho’;
     4)   UPDATE USER SET City = ‘Maho’ WHERE City = ‘Mahawa’;
     5)   UPDATE USER SET City = ‘Maho’ WHERE City != ‘Mahawa’;

     Answer : 4 )

     Let’s go through the options given / ,nd § we;s úl,am yryd huq'

     Option / úl,amh 1)
     Incorrect. MODIFY is not a valid SQL keyword for updating records.
     jerÈ fõ' MODIFY hkq jd¾;d hdj;ald,Sk lsÍu i|yd j,x.= SQL uQ, mohla fkdfõ'

     jerÈ fõ' fuys MODIFY iy INTO Ndú;h jerÈh'

     Option/ úl,amh 3)
     Incorrect. The INTO clause is not used in an UPDATE statement.
     jerÈ fõ' INTO j.ka;sh UPDATE lsÍfï m%ldYhl Ndú;d fkdfõ'

     Option/ úl,amh 4)
     Correct. The UPDATE command is used to modify existing records. The SET keyword is used to specify
     the new value for the City column, and the WHERE clause ensures that only records with 'Mahawa' in the
     City column are updated to 'Maho'.
     ksjerÈhs' UPDATE úOdkh oekg mj;sk jd¾;d fjkia lsÍug Ndú;d lrhs' k.r ;Srej i|yd kj
     w.h kshu lsÍug SET uQ, moh Ndú;d lrk w;r" WHERE j.ka;sh city ;Srefõ 'Mahawa' iys;
     jd¾;d muKla 'Maho' f,i hdj;ald,Sk lrk nj iy;sl lrhs'

     Option/ úl,amh 5)
     Incorrect. This statement will update all records where the City is not 'Mahawa', setting those cities to
     'Maho'. This is not the intended behavior, as it will change cities other than 'Mahawa'.
     jerÈ fõ' fuu m%ldYh k.rh 'Mahawa' fkdjk ish¨u jd¾;d hdj;ald,Sk lr tu k.r 'Maho' f,i
     ilihs' th 'Mahawa' yer wfkl=;a k.r fjkia lrk neúka fuh wfmalaIs; yeisÍu fkdfõ'

 37. Which of the following lists the given SQL statement clauses in the correct order?
     § we;s SQL m%ldY mo ksjerÈj fm< fm<.iajd we;af;a ljrl o@

     1)   SELECT,     FROM,           WHERE,             GROUP BY,     HAVING
     2)   SELECT,     GROUP BY,       HAVING,            FROM,         WHERE
     3)   SELECT,     HAVING,         FROM,              WHERE,        GROUP BY
     4)   SELECT,     WHERE,          GROUP BY,          HAVING,       FROM
     5)   SELECT,     WHERE,          HAVING,            GROUP BY,     FROM

     Answer : 1 )

     In SQL, the correct order of clauses in a SELECT statement is crucial for the query to function properly.
     The correct order is as follows,
     SQL ys" úuiqu ksis f,i l%shd;aul ùug SELECT m%ldYhl j.ka;sj, ksjerÈ wkqms<sfj, b;d jeo.;a
     fõ' ksjerÈ wkqms<sfj, my; mßÈ fõ"

     -    SELECT
          This clause specifies which columns you want to retrieve. It comes first.
          fuu j.ka;sh Tng ,nd .ekSug wjYH ;Sre kshu lrhs' th uq,skau meñfKa'

     -    FROM
          This clause specifies the table from which to retrieve the data. It follows the SELECT clause.
          fuu j.ka;sh o;a; ,nd .; hq;= j.=j kshu lrhs' th SELECT j.ka;sh wkq.ukh lrhs'

     -    WHERE
          This clause is used to filter records based on specified conditions. It comes after FROM.
          fuu j.ka;sh ksYaÑ; fldkafoais u; mokïj jd¾;d f;aÍug' Ndú;d lrhs' th FROM g miqj
          fhfoa'

     -    GROUP BY
          This clause groups rows that have the same values into summary rows, like finding the sum or average.
          It follows WHERE if used.
          fuu j.ka;sh tl;=j fyda idudkHh fidhd .ekSu jeks iudk w.hka we;s fma<s idrdxY fma<sj,g
          ldKav lrhs' th WHERE g miqj fhfoa'

     -    HAVING
          This clause is used to filter the results of GROUP BY after grouping is done. It comes after GROUP BY.
          fuu j.ka;sh iuQy.; lsÍfuka miq GROUP BY ys m%;sM, fmÍug Ndú;d lrhs' th GROUP BY
          g miqj meñfKa'

     The answer that gives the correct sequence is, 1)
     ksjerÈ wkqms<sfj, iys; ms<s;=r jkafka" 1) fõ'

    def list_operation(nlist):
        for i in range(len(nlist)):
            if i %+ 2 ==0:
                nlist[i] = nlist[i] ** 2
            else:
                nlist[i] = nlist[i] + 3
        return nlist
    numbers=[1,2,3,4,5]

 45. Which of the following can be used to change the look of an entire website by changing just one file?
     tla f.dkqjla muKla fjkia lr" iïmQ¾K fjí wvúhlu fmkqu fjkia lsÍug my; ljrla Ndú;
     l< yelso@

    A – external CSS / ndysr CSS
    B – inline CSS / fma<s.; CSS
    C – internal CSS / wNHka;ßl CSS

    1) A only.          2) A and B only.     3) A and C only.     4) B and C only.     5) All of the above.
       A muKs.             A yd B muKs.         A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.         by; ish,a,u.

    Answer : 1 )

    Explanation of each option / tla tla úl,amh meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    External CSS / ndysr CSS
    •   This is true. External CSS refers to a separate .css file linked to multiple HTML files of a website
        using the <link> tag in the <head> section.
        fuh i;H fõ' ndysr CSS hkq <head> fldgfia we;s <link> Wiq,kh Ndú;fhka fjí wvúhl
        nyq HTML f.dkq fj; iïnkaO lr we;s fjku .css f.dkqjls'
    •   Updating this single CSS file updates the styles of all the HTML pages linked to it.
        fuu ;ks CSS f.dkqj hdj;ald,Sk lsÍu thg iïnkaO ish¨u HTML msgq j, ú,dis;d hdj;ald,Sk
        lrhs'
        Example / WodyrKh:
        <link rel="stylesheet" href="styles.css">

    Inline CSS / fma<s.; CSS
    •   This is false. Inline CSS applies styles directly to individual HTML elements using the style attribute.
        fuh wi;H fõ' Inline CSS ú,di Wm,laIKh Ndú;d lrñka ;ks HTML uQ,dx. i|yd fl,skau
        ú,dis;d fhdohs'
    •   Since inline CSS is embedded within the element, it requires modifying each element separately,
        making it impossible to control the styling of the entire website from a single file.
        fma<s.; CSS uQ,dx.h ;=<g ldje§ we;s neúka" thg tla tla uQ,dx. fjk fjku fjkia lsÍu
        wjYH jk w;r" tuÕska iïmQ¾K fjí wvúfha ú,dis;d ;ks f.dkqjlska md,kh l< fkdyel'
        Example / WodyrKh:
        <h1 style="color: blue;">Hello</h1>

    Internal CSS / wNHka;ßl CSS
    •   This is false. Internal CSS is defined within single HTML file using <style> tag in the <head>
        section.
        fuh wi;H fõ' <head> fldgfia <style> Wiq,kh Ndú;fhka ;ks HTML f.dkqjla ;=<
        wNHka;ßl CSS w¾: olajd we;'
    •   This method only affects the styles of the specific HTML page where it is included. Changes to an
        internal CSS block will not impact other pages on the website.
        fuu l%uh n,mdkafka th we;=<;a lr we;s úfYaIs; HTML msgqfõ ú,dihkag muKs' wNHka;ßl
        CSS íf,dla tll fjkialï fjí wvúfha wfkl=;a msgq j,g n,mdkafka ke;'

    External CSS is the only method that allows global changes to the style of an entire website by editing a
    single file, whereas inline and internal CSS apply styles only at the element or page level, making them
    unsuitable for global changes.
    ndysr CSS hkq ;ks f.dkqjla ixialrKh lsÍu u.ska iïmQ¾K fjí wvúhl ú,dihg f.da,Sh
    fjkialï j,g bv fok tlu l%uh jk w;r" fma<s.; iy wNHka;ßl CSS ú,di uQ,øjH fyda msgq

 46. For what purpose is POST used in an HTML form?
     HTML fmdaruhl POST Ndú; lsÍfï wruqK l=ula o@

    1) to display a confirmation message after form submission.
       fmdaruh bÈßm;a (submit) l< miq ;yjqre lsÍfï (confirmation) mKsúvhla ixo¾Ykh lsÍug
    2) to display form data on screen
       fmdarufha o;a; ;srfha ixo¾Ykh lsÍug
    3) to refresh the web page
       fjí msgqj mqnqÿ lsÍug (refresh)
    4) to retrieve data from the server
       fiajdodhlfhka (server) o;a; ,nd.ekSug
    5) to send from data to the server
       fmdarufha o;a; fiajdodhlhg heùug

    Answer : 5 )

    Explanation of each option / tla tla úl,amh meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    Option / úl,am 1)
    This is false. While a confirmation message may be displayed after form submission, it depends on how
    the server processes the request and the response it sends back. The POST method itself does not handle
    confirmation messages.
    fïl wi;H fõ' fmdaruh bÈßm;a lsÍfuka miq ;yjqre lsÍfï mKsúvhla o¾Ykh úh yels w;r" th
    fiajdodhlh b,a,Su ilik wdldrh iy th wdmiq tjk m%;spdrh u; r|d mj;S' POST l%uhu
    ;yjqre lsÍfï mKsúv yiqrejkafka ke;'

    Option / úl,am 2)
    This is false. POST is used to send data to the server, not to display it on the screen. Displaying data
    requires additional server-side logic or client-side scripts.
    fïl wi;H fõ' POST Ndú;d lrkafka fiajdodhlhg o;a; heùug ñi th ;srh u; fmkaùug fkdfõ'
    o;a; ixo¾YKh lsÍu i|yd wu;r fiajdodhl-md¾Yùh ;¾lkh fyda fiajdodhl-md¾Yùh ial%smaÜ
    wjYH fõ'

    Option / úl,am 3)
    This is false. POST does not inherently refresh a webpage. Refreshing occurs if the server's response
    instructs the browser to do so.
    fïl wi;H fõ' POST iycfhkau fjí msgqjla kejqï fkdlrhs' fiajdodhlfha m%;spdrh n%jqirhg tfia
    lsÍug Wmfoia fokafka kï kejqï lsÍu isÿfõ'

    Option / úl,am 4)
    This is false. Retrieving data from the server is typically done using the GET method, not POST. POST is
    primarily for sending data to the server.
    fïl wi;H fõ' fiajdodhlfhka o;a; ,nd .ekSu idudkHfhka isÿ lrkq ,nkafka POST fkdj GET
    l%uh Ndú;d lrñks' POST uQ,sl jYfhka fiajdodhlh fj; o;a; heùu i|yd fõ'

    Option / úl,am 5)
    This is true. The POST method is specifically designed to send data from the client to the server in a secure
    and structured way, often for processing or storing in a database.
    fuh i;H fõ' POST l%uh úfYaIfhka ks¾udKh lr we;af;a fiajdodhlhdf.ka o;a; wdrlaIs; iy
    jHqy.; wdldrfhka fiajdodhlh fj; heùug" fndfyda úg o;a; iuqodh ;=< ieliSu fyda .nvd lsÍu
    i|yd h'

    So, the answer is, Option 5) to send from data to the server.
    tuksid" ms<s;=r jkafka úl,am 5) fmdarufha o;a; fiajdodhlhg heùug fõ'

    Explanation of each statement / tla tla m%ldYh meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    Statement / m%ldYh P:
    This is true. A classical bit is binary, representing either 0 or 1. A qubit, due to superposition, can exist as
    0, 1, or a combination of both, significantly increasing its potential states and contributing to quantum
    computing's efficiency.
    fuh i;H fõ' iïNdjH ìgqjla oaúuh jk w;r th 0 fyda 1 ksfhdackh lrhs' iqmsß ia:dk.; lsÍu

    Statement / m%ldYh Q:
    This is true. Quantum computers excel in certain tasks (ex: factoring large numbers, solving complex
    optimization problems) at speeds unattainable by classical computers.
    fuh i;H fõ' lafjdkagï mß.Kl iïNdjH mß.Klj,g w;a lr.; fkdyels fõ.hlska we;eï
    ld¾hhkays ^Wod( úYd, ixLHd idOllrKh" ixlS¾K m%Yia;lrK .eg¨ úi£u& úYsIag fõ'

    Qubits' ability to represent and process vast amounts of information simultaneously directly enables

    So, P provides the foundational reason for Q.

    mß.Klj,g weoysh fkdyels fõ.hlska iïNdjH mß.Kl i|yd l< fkdyels .Kkh lsÍï isÿ

    PHP forms allow users to input data through web pages and send it to a server for processing. These forms
    typically use the HTML tags such as <form>, along with various input types like text fields, checkboxes,
    radio buttons, and submit buttons. When a user submits a form, the data is sent to a server-side script for
    processing.
    PHP fmdaru uÕska mßYS,lhskag fjí msgq yryd o;a; wdodkh lr ieliSu i|yd fiajdodhlhlg
    heùug bv i,ihs' fuu fmdaru idudkHfhka <form> jeks HTML Wiq,k fukau text fields, checkboxes,
    radio buttons iy submit buttons jeks úúO wdodk j¾. Ndú;d lrhs' mßYS,lfhl= fmdaruhla bÈßm;a
    lrk úg" o;a; ieliSu i|yd server side script tllg hjkq ,efí'

    • GET Method:
    The GET method appends form data into the URL in name/value pairs. It allows easy sharing or
    bookmarking since all the data is visible in the URL. However, because data is visible in the URL, it's not
    secure for sensitive information, such as passwords.
    GET l%uh uÕska URL tlg kdu$w.h hq., jYfhka fmdaru o;a; tl;= lrhs' ish¨u o;a; URL
    tfla oDYHudk jk neúka th myiqfjka fnod .ekSug fyda msgq i,l=Kq lsÍug bv i,ihs' flfia
    fj;;a" URL tfla o;a; oDYHudk jk neúka" uqrmo jeks ixfõ§ f;dr;=re i|yd th wdrlaIs;
    fkdfõ'

    • POST Method:
    The POST method sends data in the body of the HTTP request, not in the URL. This makes it more secure
    for transmitting sensitive information. It’s also better for sending large amounts of data. POST is the
    preferred method for most form submissions where user privacy is important.
    POST l%uh uÕska URL tfla fkdj HTTP b,a,Sfï wka;¾.;h ;=< o;a; hjhs' fuh ixfõ§ f;dr;=re
    iïfm%aIKh lsÍu i|yd jvd;a wdrlaIs; lrhs' úYd, o;a; m%udKhla heùu i|yd o th jvd iqÿiqh'
    mßYS,l fm!oa.,sl;ajh jeo.;a jk fmdaru i|yd POST jvd;a iqÿiq l%uhhs'

 (iii) What is the benefit of using input type="email" for the email address entry field instead of
       input type="text"?
       b-;eme,a ,smskh we;=<;a lrk lafIa;%h i|yd input type=“text” fjkqjg input
       type=“email” Ndú; lsÍfï jdish l=ulao@

     Using type="email" instead of type="text" in a PHP form input offers several important advantages,
     especially in terms of user experience and data validation. When a field is set to type="email", modern
     browsers perform automatic client-side validation. This means they check whether the input follows the
     standard email format (e.g., contains "@" and a domain like ".com") before the form can be submitted.
     This reduces the number of incorrect or invalid entries being sent to the server.
     PHP fmdaru wdodkhl type="text" fjkqjg type="email" Ndú;d lsÍu jeo.;a jdis lsysmhla

     This pattern is used in an HTML input field to validate that the user enters exactly 10 digits, with no
     spaces, letters, or symbols.

     - {10} means the user can enter only 10 digits. Input with more or fewer digits will not be accepted.
     - {10} hkafkka woyia lrkafka mßYS,lhdg we;=<;a l< yelafla b,lalï 10la muKla njhs' jeä
     fyda wvq b,lalï iys; wdodkh ms<s.kq fkd,efí'

     especially useful in contact forms, registration pages, or any field that requires a specific phone number

 (vi) Explain the main purpose of the HTML code extract shown in Figure 1.3.
      rEmh 1.3 ka fmkajk fla; fldgfia uq,sl wruqK l=ulao@


        <?php
          $host = "localhost";
          $db_user = "student_user";
          $db_password = "student_pass";
          $db_name = "studentDB";
          $conn = mysqli_connect ($host, $db_user, $db_password,
          $db_name);
          if (!$conn) {
            die("<tr><td colspan='3'>Connection failed:" .
            mysqli_connect_error () . "</td></tr>");
            }
          $sql = "SELECT student_id, first_name, last_name FROM stu-
          dents";
          $result = mysqli_query ($conn, $sql) ;
          if (mysqli_num_rows ($result) > 0) {
            while ($row = mysqli_fetch_assoc ($result) ) {
              echo "<tr>
                   <td>" . $row["student_id"] . "</td>
                   <td>" . $row["first_name"] . "</td>
                   <td>" . $row["last_name"] . "</td>
                   </tr>";
            }
          } else {
            echo "<tr><td colspan='3'>No students found .</td></tr>";
          }
          mysqli_close ($conn);
        ?>

    credentials with the mysqli_connect function.

2.(a)(i) Following diagram shows the abstract model of information creation:
         my; rEm igykska fmkajkafka f;dr;=re ks¾udKh lsrsfï úhqla; wdlD;shls'

          A                  B                    C

      Identify the A, B and C above.
      by; A, B iy C yÿkd.kak'

      A: Input/data / wdodkh$o;a;
      B: Process/processing / l%shdj,sh$ieliSu
      C: Output/information / m%;sodkh$f;dr;=re

      Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu:

      The abstract model of information creation is often represented as a 3-step process involving Data,
      Processing, and Information.
      f;dr;=re ks¾udKfha úhqla; wdlD;sh fndfyda úg o;a;" ieliSu iy f;dr;=re we;=<;a mshjr 3
      l%shdj,shla f,i ksrEmKh flf¾'

      Input/ Data / wdodkh$ o;a;
      Raw facts and figures that have no meaning by themselves.
      ;ksju w¾:hla fkdue;s wuq lreKq iy ixLHd.
      Examples: Numbers, names, dates, sensor readings.
      WodyrK( wxl" kï" Èkhka" ixfõol lshùï'
      Input or data is not yet useful until it is processed.
      wdodkh fyda o;a; ielfik ;=re m%fhdackj;a fkdfõ'

      Process/ processing / l%shdj,sh$ ieliSu
      The action or method used to convert data into meaningful form.
      o;a; w¾:j;a wdldrhlg mßj¾;kh lsÍug Ndú;d lrk l%shdj fyda l%uh'
      May involve organizing, calculating, sorting, classifying, or summarizing.
      ixúOdkh lsÍu" .Kkh lsÍu" j¾. lsÍu" j¾.SlrKh lsÍu fyda idrdxY lsÍu we;=<;a úh yelsh'
      This is the transformation stage / fuh mßj¾;k wjêhhs.

      Output/ information / m%;sodkh$ f;dr;=re
      Processed data that is meaningful and useful for decision-making.
      ;SrK .ekSu i|yd w¾:j;a iy m%fhdackj;a jk ieliQ o;a;'
      Information is the final output of the process.
      f;dr;=re hkq l%shdj,sfha wjidk m%;sodkhhs'
      Example: "Sales increased by 15% in May" (after processing sales data).
      WodyrKh( “uehs udifha§ úl=Kqï 15% lska jeä úh’ ^úl=Kqï o;a; ieliSfuka miq&'

    • One buyer requests a product or service / tla .ekqïlrefjl= ksIamdokhla fyda fiajdjla b,a,d isà'

 (b) Write down the output of the following Python code.
     my; mhs;ka fla;fha m%;sodkh ,shd olajkak'

    def check_values (n) :
          result = []
          for i in range(1, n + 1):
                if i % 2 == 0 and i % 3 == 0:
                      result.append (i)
          return result
    output = check_values (12)
    print (output)

    Function Definition/ Y%s; w¾: oelaùu:
    def check_values(n):

    result = []

        hï fldkafoaishla imqrd,k w.hka .nvd lsÍu i|yd ,ehsia;= kï l< m%;sM,hla ks¾udKh fõ'

    Loop Through Numbers from 1 to n (inclusive)

    for i in range(1, n + 1):

         •   range(1, n + 1) means the loop will run for values from 1 to n (which is 12), so
             mrdih range(1, n + 1) hkafkka woyia lrkafka Æmh 1 isg n olajd w.hka i|yd l%shd;aul
             jk njhs (th 12) tneúka:

     if i % 2 == 0 and i % 3 == 0:

 (c) Fill the five blanks ^----------------------& of the following Python code which has been written to find
     the prime numbers from 2 upto a given number.
     fofla isg fok ,o ixLHdjla f;la we;s m%:ul ixLHd ^prime numbers& m%;sodkh lsÍug ,shd we;s
     my; mhs;ka fla;fha ysia;eka ^-------------& my mqrjkak'

     Note: A prime number is any whole number greater than 1 that is divisible only by 1 and itself. e.g.,
           Prime numbers from 2 up to number 5 are 2, 3 and 5.
           m%:ul ixLHdjla hkq 1ka iy tu ixLHdfjkau muKla fnÈh yels 1g jeä ´kEu mq¾K
           ixLHdjls' Wod: fofla isg my f;la we;s m%:ul ixLHd jkafka 2, 3 iy 5 fõ'

     upper = int (input ('Enter end of range:'))
     if upper > 1:
           print ("Prime numbers between 2 and ", upper, "are:")
           for num in range (2, upper + 1) :
                 for i in range (2, num) :
                       if (num % i) == 0 :
                             break
                 else:
                    print (num)

     The outer loop goes through numbers from 2 to the upper limit.
     msg; Æmh 2 isg by< iSudj olajd ixLHd yryd .uka lrhs'

     The inner loop checks if num has any divisor other than 1 and itself.
     wNHka;r Æmh wxlhg 1 iy th yer fjk;a Ndclhla ;sfíoehs mÍlaId lrhs'
     If a divisor is found → not prime → break./ Ndclhla yuq jqjfyd;a → m%:ul fkdfõ → wjika lsÍu

 (ii) When making the final bill, a 10% service charge is added to the total amount that the guest has to
      pay. Write down the process description for '2. Make final bill'.
      wjidk ì, idok úg wuq;a;d f.úh hq;= uq¿ uqo,g 10% l fiajd noaola tl;= flf¾' ‘2. wjidk
      Å, idokak’ ioyd ls%hdj,s úia;rh (process description) ,shd olajkak'

     Total cost = room cost of guest

     DO WHILE there are restaurant transactions by guests
         Total cost = Total cost + restaurant cost
     END WHILE
     DO WHILE there are health center transactions by guests
         Total cost = Total cost + health center cost
     END WHILE
     Total cost = Total cost + (Total cost * 0.1)

(b)(i) Explain the use of half adder in digital circuits.
       wxls; mßm:j, w¾Odl,lfha (half adder) Ndú;h myokak'

                                             A half adder is a basic digital circuit used for adding two single-bit
                                             binary numbers, producing a sum and a carry-out as outputs. It forms
                                             the foundation for building more complex circuits like full adders.
                                             w¾Odl,lh hkq ;ks ìgq oaúuh ixLHd folla tl;= lsÍu i|yd
                                             Ndú;d lrk uQ,sl wxls; mßm:hls" m%;sodkhka f,i sum iy
                                             carry-out ksmojhs' th mQ¾Kdl,l jeks jvd;a ixlS¾K mßm:
                                             ;ekSu i|yd moku idohs'

  (ii) Describe how a flip flop works as a memory element in digital circuits. Explain how it differs from
       combinational logic gates.
       wxls; mßm:j, u;l uQ,hka f,i ms<sfmd<la (flip flop) l%shdlrk whqre úia;r lrkak' th ixfhdack
       ;¾lk oajdrj,ska (combinational logic gates) fjkia jk whqre myokak'

     A flip-flop is a type of digital circuit used to store a single bit of data, either a 0 or a 1. It acts as a memory
     element, meaning it can keep its value even after the input stops. It changes its state (from 0 to 1 or 1 to
     0) when it receives a clock signal, making it useful in situations where you need to keep track of a value
     or sequence over time.
     ms<sfmd< hkq 0 fyda 1hk tla o;a; ìgqjla .nvd lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d lrk wxls; mßm: j¾.hls'
     th u;l uQ,hla f,i l%shd lrhs" tkï wdodkh keje;aùfuka miqj mjd tys w.h ;nd .; yel' th
     ld, ix{djla ,enqKq úg tys ;;a;ajh ^0 isg 1 fyda 1 isg 0 olajd& fjkia lrhs" ld,h;a iuÕ
     w.hla fyda wkqms<sfj,la ksÍlaIKh lsÍug wjYH wjia:djkays§ th m%fhdackj;a fõ'

     Flip-flops are different from combinational logic gates because these gates produce outputs immediately
     based on inputs, without storing any value.
     ms<sfmd<, ixfhdack ;d¾lsl oajdrj,g jvd fjkia jkafka fuu oajdr lsisÿ w.hla .nvd fkdlr" wdodk
     u; mokïj jydu m%;sodkhka ksmojk neúks'

      using a twisted pair Ethernet cable.
      Rcq ,laIH ia:,lhlg (point-to-point topology) wkqj" mß.Klhla iy uqølhla weUß hq., B;¾fkÜ

     Point-to-point topology is a type of topology that works on the functionality of the sender and receiver. It
     is the simplest communication between two nodes, in which one is the sender and the other one is the
     receiver. Point-to-Point provides high bandwidth.

     There's a dedicated connection between the computer and the printer.

     Data flows directly between the two devices without passing through any intermediate devices like a hub
     or switch.
     kdNshla fyda iaúphla jeks lsisÿ w;rueÈ Wmdx. yryd .uka fkdlr iDcqju Wmdx. fol w;r o;a;

     This setup is ideal for short distances and when only two devices need to communicate.

     Switches / iaúp:

(d)(i) What is the role of the Domain Name System (DNS) when the user enters a web address
       (e.g., http://www.gmail.com) into the URL field of a web browser?
       mßYS,lfhl= fjí w;ßlaiqjl (URL) lafIa;%hg fjí ,smskhla (Wod' http://www.gmail.com) we;=<;a
       l< úg" jiï kdu moaO;sfha (DNS ys) ld¾hNdrh l=ula o@

     When a user enters a web address, such as http://www.gmail.com, into a browser, the Domain Name
     System (DNS) plays a role in resolving the domain name into an IP address, which is essential for
     establishing communication with the target web server.
     mßYS,lfhl= http://www.gmail.com jeks fjí ,smskhla fjí w;ßlaiqjlg we;=¿ l< úg" jiï kduh
     IP ,smskhlg úi£fï ld¾hNdrhla jiï kdu moaO;sh (DNS) bgq lrhs" th b,lal.; fjí
     fiajdodhlh iuÕ ikaksfõokh ia:dms; lsÍu i|yd w;HjYH fõ'

     DNS translates the human-readable domain name (www.gmail.com) into a machine-readable IP address
     (e.g., 142.250.190.165). This is because devices on the internet communicate using IP addresses, not
     domain names.
     DNS u.ska ñksiqkag lshúh yels jiï kduh (www.gmail.com) hka;%fhka lshúh yels IP ,smskhla
     njg mßj¾;kh lrhs (Wod(142.250.190.165). fuhg fya;=j wka;¾cd,fha we;s Wmdx. jiï kdu
     fkdj IP ,smsk Ndú;fhka ikaksfõokh lsÍuhs'

     The DNS acts as a distributed database that maps domain names to their corresponding IP addresses,
     enabling efficient and user-friendly access to internet resources. Without DNS, users would need to
     remember numerical IP addresses for every website, which would be impractical.
     DNS uÕska jiï kdu tAjdfha wkqrEm IP ,smskj,g is;shï.; lrk fnod yßk ,o o;a; iuqodhla
     f,i l%shd lrk w;r tuÕska wka;¾cd, iïm;a fj; ld¾hlaIu iy mßYS,l ys;ldó m%fõYhla ,nd

     The hierarchical and distributed structure of the DNS (Domain Name System) refers to the way the system
     is organized and operates to efficiently resolve domain names into IP addresses across the global internet.
     DNS (jiï kdu moaO;sh) ys OQrdj,s iy úia;D; jHqyh hkq moaO;sh ixúOdkh lr we;s wdldrh

     wdldrhhs'

     The hierarchical structure of DNS ensures logical and organized resolution of domain names, while the
     distributed structure ensures scalability, reliability, and efficiency. Together, these features make the DNS
     a robust and indispensable system for managing internet traffic.

 (f) Suppose Kamal wants to send the secret message ADD to Nimal. Kamal converts ADD to CEE before

  (i) Write down the encryption key used by Kamal in this communication.
      fuu ikaksfõokfha lu,a Ndú;d l< fla;k h;=r (encryption key) ,shd olajkak'

     To convert the secret message, ADD to CEE, Kamal is using a substitution cipher, where each letter of
     the plaintext is replaced by another letter.
     ryia mKsúvh ADD, CEE njg mßj¾;kh lsÍu i|yd" lu,a wdfoaYl fla;dxlhla Ndú;d lrhs"
     tys§ ir, mdGfhys iEu wl=rlau fjk;a wl=rlska m%;sia:dmkh fõ'

     Step-by-Step Explanation / mshjfrka mshjr meyeÈ,s lsÍu

     •   Original message / uq,a mKsúvh: ADD

     •   Encrypted message / fla;kh l< mKsúvh: CEE

     Now, let's look at the letter-by-letter transformation,
     oeka" wl=frka wl=r mßj¾;kh foi n,uq"

     A → C: A is substituted with C.
     A → C: A C iuÕ wdfoaY lr we;'

     D → E: D is substituted with E.
     D → E: D E iuÕ wdfoaY lr we;'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -86-                                          #ictfromabc


    D → E: D is substituted with E again.
    D → E: D kej;;a E iuÕ wdfoaY lr we;'

    This indicates that Kamal has shifted each letter by +2 positions in the alphabet. Specifically
    fuhska weÕfjkafka lu,a fydaäfha iEu wl=rlau +2 ia:dkj,ska udre lr we;s njhs' úfYaIfhka

    - A → C (shifted by 2)
    - A → C (2 ka udre lr we;)

    - D → E (shifted by 1)
    - D → E (1 ka udre lr we;)

    - D → E (shifted by 1 again)
    - D → E (kej; 1 ka udre lr we;)

    Thus, the encryption key Kamal used appears to be a shift of +2 for the first letter, and a shift of +1 for
    the subsequent letters.
    fï wkqj" lu,a Ndú;d l< fla;dxlk h;=r m<uq wl=r i|yd +2 udrejla iy B<Õ wl=re i|yd
    +1 udrejla f,i fmfka'

    Final Answer / wjidk ms<s;=r
    The encryption key used by Kamal in this communication is,
    fuu ikaksfõokfha§ lu,a Ndú;d lrk fla;dxlk h;=r jkafka"

    Shift of +2 for the first letter
    m<uq wl=r i|yd +2 udre lrkak (A → C)

    Shift of +1 for the second and third letters (D → E, D → E)
    fojk iy f;jk wl=re i|yd +1 udre lrkak (D → E, D → E)

      mßm:fha Ndú; lrk ikafõolfha (sensor) yeisÍu iyd l%shdldß;ajh myokak'

     The sensor used in the circuit appears to be a magnetic reed switch. This sensor operates based on the
     presence or absence of a magnetic field. When the door is closed, the magnet remains close to the sensor,
     keeping the circuit inactive. However, when the door opens, the magnet moves away, causing the sensor
     to send a HIGH signal to the microcontroller (ATmega328P). The microcontroller detects this state change
     and activates the buzzer to produce a sound, signaling that the door has been opened.
     mßm:fha Ndú;d lrk ixfõolh pqïnl reed iaúphla f,i fmfka' fuu ixfõolh pqïNl lafIa;%hla

 (ii) To operate this setup Saman wrote the firmware code shown in Figure 7.2. However, the setup failed to

      fuu mßm:h l%shdlrùug iuka rEmh 7.2 ys oelafjk iaÒrdx. (firmware) fla;h ,sõfõh' tfy;a

                       Figure / rEmh 7.2

    imhd we;s fla;h ksis f,i l%shd;aul ùu je<elaùfï m%Odk .eg¿ folla we;'

    •    The tone() function is incorrectly applied to the sensor pin (SensorP) instead of the buzzer pin

   The corrected code is as follows / ksjerÈ lrk ,o fla;h my; mßÈ fõ:

   const int SensorP = 5;
   const int BuzzerP = 8;

   void setup() {
       pinMode(SensorP, INPUT);
       pinMode(BuzzerP, OUTPUT);
   }

   void loop() {
       int senState = digitalRead(SensorP);
       if (senState == HIGH)
           tone(BuzzerP, 262); //Corrected to use the buzzer pin (BuzzerP)
       else
           noTone(BuzzerP);    //Corrected to stop the tone on the buzzer pin (BuzzerP)
   }

8.(a) Write the output of the Python code given in Figure 8.1.
      rEmh 8.1 ys oelafjk mhs;ka fla;fha m%;sodkh ,shd olajkak'

         def calculate(n):
             result = 0
             for i in range(1,n+1):
                 for j in range(i):
                     result += i*j
             return result

         print(calculate(4))
                  Figure / rEmh 8.1

     Answer / ms<s;=r: 35

     Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu:
     The Python code calculates the result of nested loops where for each i (from 1 to n), a loop iterates j
     times (from 0 to i-1) and the value i * j is added to result.
     mhs;ka fla;h u.ska tla tla i ^ 1 isg n olajd& i|yd j w.hka ^0 isg i - 1 olajd& mqkrdj¾;kh
     jk w;r i * j w.h m%;sM,h (result) i|yd tl;= lrk w;r tu.ska m%;sM,h (result) .Kkh lrhs'

     •    i = 1 → j loop runs from 0 to 0 (only 1 iteration) / Æmh 0 isg 0 olajd Odjkh fõ ^1
          mqkrdj¾;khla muKs& :
          result += 1 * 0 = 0 → result = 0

     •    i = 2 → j loop runs from 0 to 1 (2 iterations) / Æmh 0 isg 1 olajd Odjkh fõ ^mqkrdj¾;k 2la
          muKs&:
          result += 2 * 0 = 0 → result = 0
          result += 2 * 1 = 2 → result = 2

     •    i = 3 → j loop runs from 0 to 2 (3 iterations) / Æmh 0 isg 2 olajd Odjkh fõ ^mqkrdj¾;k 3la
          muKs&:
          result += 3 * 0 = 0 → result = 2
          result += 3 * 1 = 3 → result = 5
          result += 3 * 2 = 6 → result = 11

     •    i = 4 → j loop runs from 0 to 3 (4 iterations) / Æmh 0 isg 3 olajd Odjkh fõ ^mqkrdj¾;kh 4la
          muKs&:
          result += 4 * 0 = 0 → result = 11
          result += 4 * 1 = 4 → result = 15
          result += 4 * 2 = 8 → result = 23
          result += 4 * 3 = 12 → result = 35

 (b) Figure 8.2 contains a labeled Python code to print the binary equivalent of an input decimal whole number.
     Write down the suitable replacements for the labels P-U to complete the code.
     rEmh 8.2 ys oelafjk f,an, iys; mhs;ka fla;h wdodkh l< oYuh mQ¾K ixLHdjlg ;=,H oaùuh
     ixLHdj uqøKh lrhs fla;h iïmQ¾K lsÍu i|yd P-U f,an,j,g iqÿiq wdfoaYl ,shd olajkak

      reversed_binary = ""

      n = float(input("Enter a whole number:"))
      if n % 1 != P:
          exit("Please enter a whole number.")
      n = Q(n) #convert n to an integer
      if (n == P):
          print(n)

      while n >= 1:
          reversed_binary = reversed_binary + R (S)
          n = T
      binary = U[::-1]
      print(binary)
                       Figure / rEmh 8.2

     Answers / ms<s;=re:
     P: 0                             Q: int                           R: str
     S: n % 2                         T: n // 2                        U: reversed_binary

     Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu:

      P: 0                  →      To check if the remainder is not 0 (not a whole number)
                                   b;sßh 0 fkdfõoehs mÍlaId lsÍug ^iïmQ¾K ixLHdjla fkdfõ&.
      Q: int                →      Convert the floating-point number to an integer

      R: str                →      Convert the binary digit (remainder) to a string for concatenation /
                                   oaúuh b,lalï ^fYaIh& ixhqla; lsÍu i|yd string tllg
                                   mßj¾;kh lsÍu.
      S: n % 2              →      Compute the remainder when n is divided by 2
                                   2 ka fn¥ úg b;sßh .Kkh lsÍu.
      T: n // 2             →      Perform integer division to update n
                                   hdj;ald,Sk lsÍug mQ¾K ixLHd fn§u isÿ lsÍu.
      U: reversed_binary    →      Use the reversed binary string
                                   reversed binary Ndú;d lsÍu.

     Final code / wjidk fla;h:
      reversed_binary = ""
      n = float(input("Enter a whole number:"))
      if n % 1 != 0:
          exit("Please enter a whole number.")
      n = int(n)
      if ( n == 0 ):
          print(n)

      while n >= 1:
          reversed_binary = reversed_binary + str( n % 2 )
          n = n // 2
      binary = reversed_binary[::-1]
      print(binary)

    •   If the number entered is 0, the program immediately outputs 0 because the binary representation of 0
        is simply 0.

        0 m%;sodkh lrhs'

    Binary Conversion Logic / oaúuh mßj¾;k ;¾lkh
    while n >= 1:
        reversed_binary = reversed_binary + str(n % 2)
        n = n // 2
    •   This loop repeatedly divides the number n by 2 (integer division) and appends the remainder (n % 2)
        to the reversed_binary string.
        fuu Æmh kej; kej;;a n ixLHdj 2 ka ^mQ¾K ixLHd fn§u& fnok w;r b;sßh ^ n % 2&
        reversed_binary string tllg tl;= lrhs'
    •   As the number is divided, the remainders represent the binary digits of the number but are generated
        in reverse order.
        ixLHdj fn§ we;s úg" b;sßh ixLHdfõ oaúuh b,lalï ksfhdackh lrk kuq;a m%;sf,dau
        wkqms<sfj,ska ckkh fõ'

    Reversing the Binary String / oaúuh String tl wdmiq yerùu
    binary = reversed_binary[::-1]
    •   The [::-1] slicing operation reverses the reversed_binary string to get the correct binary representation.
        [::-1] slicing l%shdj ksjerÈ oaúuh ksrEmKh ,nd .ekSu i|yd reversed_binary string tl wdmiq
        yrjhs'

    Output / m%;sodkh

    •   The binary representation of the number is printed.
        wxlfha oaúuh ksrEmKh uqøKh lrhs'

    Example Execution / WodyrK l%shd;aul lsÍu
    1. Input / wdodkh
       Enter a whole number: 10
    2. Steps / mshjr:
       • 10 % 2 = 0, append 0 → reversed_binary = "0"
       • 10 // 2 = 5
       • 5 % 2 = 1, append 1 → reversed_binary = "01"
       • 5 // 2 = 2
       • 2 % 2 = 0, append 0 → reversed_binary = "010"
       • 2 // 2 = 1
       • 1 % 2 = 1, append 1 → reversed_binary = "0101"
       • 1 // 2 = 0 (loop terminates / Æmh wjika fõ ).

    3. Reversed Binary / m%;sf,dau oaúuh:
       • reversed_binary[::-1] gives "1010".

    4. Output / m%;sodkh
       1010

     Notes / igyka:

    Corrected code / ksjerÈ fla;h
    def item_selector(remainder, weights, values, names):
        n = len(weights)
        merged = [(values[i], weights[i], names[i], i) for i in range(n)]
        print("Merged:", merged)
        merged.sort(reverse=True, key=lambda x: x[0])
        print("Sorted records:", merged)

            res = ''
            for value, weight, name, index in merged:
                if remainder >= weight:
                    res = res + name + ' '
                    remainder = remainder - weight
            return res

    # Input:
    bag_capacity = 50
    weights = [49, 10, 35]
    values = [60, 100, 120]
    names = ["Laptop", "Book", "Clothes"]

    selected = item_selector(bag_capacity, weights, values, names)
    print("Selected items:", selected)

     Result j.=j l=uk m%u; wjia:dfjys mj;S o@ Tfí ms<s;=r idOdrKSlrKh lrkak'

     1st Normal Form / 1jk m%u; wjia:dj (1NF)

     The table satisfies 1NF because all columns contain atomic values. No repeating groups or multivalued
     attributes exist.
     ish¨u ;Sre mrudKql w.hka wvx.= jk ksid j.=j 1NF ;Dma;su;a lrhs' mqkrdj¾;k lKavdhï fyda
     nyq jákdlï iys; Wm,laIK fkdue;'
     The table does not satisfy 2NF because there are partial dependencies:
     wdxYsl mrdhla;;d we;s neúka j.=j 2NF ;Dma;su;a fkdfõ(

     •     The Teacher_Name, Teacher_ID and Subject_Name dependent on Subject_ID and not on the full
           primary key, which seems to be (Student_ID, Subject_ID).

           m%d:ñl h;=r f,i fmfkk (Student_ID, Subject_ID) u; fkdfõ'

           2NF ys" ish¨u m%d:ñl fkdjk Wm,laIK tys fldgila muKla fkdj" iïmQ¾K m%d:ñl h;=r

          tlsfkl iyfhda.fhka l%shd lrhs'

    Instruction Execution / Wmfoia l%shd;aul lsÍu(
    Instructions are executed in the fetch-decode-execute cycle:

        l%shd;aul lsÍu( CPU u.ska fufyhqu isÿ lrhs ^Wod( tl;= lsÍu&'

    Each high-level Python statement translates to multiple low-level CPU instructions.

 (b) Show that the answer for 11002 - 10102 could be obtained by adding the 2s complement of 10102 to 11002
     and ignoring the carry.
     11002 - 10102 ys ms<s;=r" 10102 ys foflys wkqmQrlh 11002 g tl;= lr" bÈßhg f.k ìgqj (carry)
     fkdi,ld yeÍfuka ,nd.; yels nj fmkajkak'

    Let’s do the calculation as we would normally do with binary numbers.
    idudkHfhka oaúuh ixLHd j,ska isÿ lrk wdldrhg .Kkh lsÍu lruq'

    11002 - 10102 = 00102 = 210

    Therefore, by adding the 2s complement of 10102 to 11002, the final as we should get would be 210.
    tneúka" 10102 ys 2 ys wkqmQrlh 11002 g tl;= lsÍfuka" wmg ,eìh hq;= wjidk w.h 210 fõ'

    Two’s complement / foflys wkqmQrlh
    The 2's complement of a binary number is obtained by:
    oaúuh ixLHdjl 2ys wkqmQrlh ,nd .kafka(
    •   Taking the 1's complement (inverting all bits).
        1 ys wkqmQrlh ,nd .ekSfuka ^ish¨ ìgq m%;sf,dau lsÍu&'
    •   Adding 1 to the result.
        iy tu m%;sM,hg 1la tl;= lsÍfuks'

    This allows subtraction to be performed as addition.
    fuu.ska tl;= lsÍula f,i wvq lsÍu isÿ lsÍug bv i,ihs'

    Since the first bit of 10102 is 1, we have to go through the above steps. If it was 0, the 2s complement
    would be the same number.
    10102 ys m<uq ìgqj 1 jk neúka by; mshjr yryd hdug isÿfõ' th 0 jQjdkï" 2 ys wkqmQrlh
    tu ixLHdju fõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -109-                                          #ictfromabc


     Converting 10102 into 2's Complement / 10102 2 ys wkqmQrlh njg mßj¾;kh lsÍu

     Find the 1's complement by flipping the bits: 10102 → 01012
     ìgq fmr<Sfuka 1 ys wkqmQrlh fiùu

     Add 1 to the 1's complement: 01012 + 12 = 01102
     1 ys wkqmQrlhg 1la tl;= lrkak

     So, the 2's complement of 10102 is 01102.
     tneúka" 10102 ys 2 ys wkqmQrlh 01102 fõ'

     Adding the two’s complement to 11002 / foflys wkqmQrlh 11002 g tl;= lsÍu

     11002 + 01102 = 100102

     So, the answer we get is 100102.
     tneúka" wmg ,efnk ms<s;=r 100102 fõ'

     To confirm if we get the correct answer, we have to ignore the first bit, which is the carry bit. By ignoring
     the carry bit, we can see that the final answer is 00102 which is indeed is 2 in decimal.
     wmg ksjerÈ ms<s;=r ,efnkafka oehs ;yjqre lr .ekSu i|yd" /f.k hdfï ìgqj jk m<uq ìgqj
     fkdi,ld yeßh hq;=h' /f.k hdfï ìgqj fkdi,ld yeÍfuka" wjidk ms<s;=r 00102 jk w;r th
     oyfha mdofhka 2 jk nj wmg oel.; yelsh

 (c) Amal starts a single processor computer and starts a web browser. After sometime he starts a spreadsheet
     application too on the same computer.
     wu,a ;ks ilikhla iys; (single processor) mß.Klhla mK.kajd fjí w;sßlaiqjla (web browser)
     wrUhs' uo fõ,djlg miq tu mß.Klfhau Tyq me;=reïm;a (spreadsheet) fhÿula o wrUhs'

  (i) READY, RUNNING and BLOCKED are three states of a process. When the operating system of the
      computer temporarily stops the above web browser process in order to let the spreadsheet process run on
      the processor, to which of the above three states will the web browser process transit?
      iQodkï" l%shd;aul iy wjysr l< hkq l%shdhkhl ;;a;aj ;=kls' mß.Klfha fufyhqï moaO;sh" by;
      me;=reïm;a l%shdhkhg ilikh u; Odjkh ùug bv ie,iSu ioyd fjí w;ßlaiq l%shdhkh
      ;djld,slj k;r lrhs' túg fjí w;ßlaiq l%shdhkh by; ;;a;aj ;=fkka l=uk ;;a;ajhg
      ixl%dka;s fõ o @

     When the operating system temporarily stops the web browser process to let the spreadsheet process run,
     the web browser process transitions to the READY state.
     me;=reïm;a l%shdj,shg l%shd;aul ùug bv §u i|yd fufyhqï moaO;sh úiska fjí w;ßlaiq l%shdj,sh
     ;djld,slj kj;ajk úg" th READY ;;a;ajhg ixl%uKh fõ'
     Reason: The READY state means the process is prepared to run but is waiting for the CPU to become
     available. The web browser process is not BLOCKED because it is not waiting for I/O or other events; it
     is simply paused to allow another process (the spreadsheet) to run.
     fya;=j( READY ;;a;ajh hkafkka woyia jkafka l%shdj,sh l%shd;aul ùug iQodkï kuq;a CPU tl
     ,nd .ekSug n,d isák ;;ajhhs' fuys§ I$O fyda fjk;a isÿùï i|yd n,d fkdisák neúka fjí n%jqir
     l%shdj,sh wjysr lr fkdue;; fjk;a l%shdj,shla ^me;=reïm;& l%shd;aul ùug bv §u i|yd th
     ;djld,slj kj;d we;'

     Explanation of the 7 states / ;;a;aj 7 meyeÈ,s lsÍu

     New: The process is being created and has not yet started execution.
     l%shdj,sh ks¾udKh fjñka mj;sk w;r ;ju;a l%shd;aul lsÍu wdrïN lr fkdue;'

     Ready: The process is ready to run but is waiting for CPU time.
     l%shdj,sh l%shd;aul ùug iQodkï kuq;a CPU ld,h ,efnk f;la n,d isà'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -110-                                         #ictfromabc


     Running: The process is currently executing on the CPU.
     l%shdj,sh oekg CPU u; l%shd;aul fõ'

     Blocked (Waiting): The process is waiting for an event (e.g., I/O completion) to proceed.
     l%shdj,sh isÿùula ^Wod( I$O iïmQ¾K lsÍu& isÿjkf;la n,d isà'

     Terminated: The process has completed execution and is no longer active.
     l%shdj,sh l%shd;aul lsÍu iïmQ¾K ù we;s w;r ;jÿrg;a th il%sh fkdfõ'

     Suspended Ready: The process is ready to run but has been moved out of main memory (to disk) to free
     up resources.
     l%shdj,sh l%shd;aul ùug iQodkï kuq;a iïm;a ksoyia lsÍug m%Odk u;lfhka ^;eáh fj;& f.k
     f.dia we;'

     Suspended Blocked: The process is both waiting for an event and has been moved out of main memory.
     l%shdj,sh isÿùula i|yd n,d isák w;r m%Odk u;lfhka msg;go f.k f.dia we;

 (ii) Write down the state transition that the web browser process will undergo, when it has to wait for some
      data from the web server.
      fjí fiajdodhlhdf.ka (web server) o;a; iuyrla ,efnk ;=re n,d isàug isÿjk úg fjí w;ßlaiq
      l%shdhkfha isÿjk wjia:d ixl%dka;sh (state transition) l=ulaoehs ,shd olajkak

     When the web browser process has to wait for data from the web server, it will transition as follows:
     fjí n%jqir l%shdj,shg fjí fiajdodhlfhka o;a; ,efnk f;la /£ isàug isÿ jQ úg" th my; mßÈ
     ixl%uKh jkq we;(

     Running → Blocked:

     The process moves from the Running state to the Blocked state because it cannot continue execution until
     the required data is received from the web server (an I/O operation or network request).
     fjí fiajdodhlfhka wjYH o;a; ,efnk ;=re ^I$O fufyhqula fyda cd, b,a,Sula& l%shd;aul lsÍu
     È.gu lrf.k hd fkdyels ksid l%shdj,sh Odjk ;;ajfha isg wjysr l< ;;ajhg .uka lrhs'

     Blocked → Ready:

     Once the data from the web server arrives, the process transitions from the Blocked state to the Ready
     state, indicating that it is ready to execute again when the CPU becomes available.
     fjí fiajdodhlfhka o;a; meñKs miq" l%shdj,sh wjysr l< ;;ajfha isg iQodkï ;;a;ajhg ixl%uKh
     fõ" tu.ska CPU tl ,nd .; yels jQ úg th kej; l%shd;aul lsÍug iQodkï nj fmkakqï lrhs'

     This transition occurs because the process is now waiting on an external event (data arrival), and the OS
     must manage resources efficiently by allowing other processes to use the CPU in the meantime.
     fuu ixl%dka;sh isÿ jkafka l%shdj,sh oeka ndysr isÿùula ^o;a; meñŒu& u; /£ isák ksid jk w;r"
     tA w;r;=r§ CPU tl Ndú;d lsÍug fjk;a l%shdj,Skag bv §fuka OS úiska iïm;a ld¾hlaIuj
     l<ukdlrKh lrhs'

 (iii) Explain the use of 'Program counter' of the Process Control Blocks during a web browser process →
       spreadsheet process context switch.
       fjí w;ßlaiq l%shdhkh → me;=reïm;a l%shdhkh hk ikao¾N iaúpfha§ (context switch)" l%shdhk
       md,k LKavj, (PCB) ‘jevigyka .Klfha (program counter)’ Ndú;h meyeÈ,s lrkak

     During a context switch, the Program Counter (PC) of the web browser process is saved in its PCB to store
     the address of the next instruction, and the Program Counter of the spreadsheet process is loaded from its
     PCB to resume its execution.
     ikao¾N iaúÉphla w;r;=r" ó<Õ Wmfofiys ,smskh .nvd lsÍu i|yd fjí n%jqir l%shdj,sfha
     l%uf,aL .Klh ^PC& tu l%shdj,sfha PCB ys iqrlsk w;r" me;=reïm;a l%shdj,sh l%shd;aul lsÍu
     kej; wdrïN lsÍu i|yd tys l%uf,aL .Klh tu l%shdj,sfha PCB fj;ska mQrKh fõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                              -111-                                         #ictfromabc


    What is the Program Counter? / l%uf,aL .Klh hkq l=ulao@
    The Program Counter holds the memory address of the next instruction to be executed in a process.
    l%uf,aL .Klh u.ska l%shdj,shl§ l%shd;aul l< hq;= B<Õ Wmfoia j, u;l ,smskh ;nd .kS'

    During the Context Switch / ikao¾Nh iaúph w;r;=r(

    Saving the current program counter of the web browser process:
    fjí n%jqir l%shdj,sfha j;auka l%uf,aL .Klh iq/lSu(

    The current value of the program counter, which points to the next instruction in the web browser process,
    is saved in its PCB.
    fjí n%jqir l%shdj,sfha B<Õ Wmfoia fj; fhduq jk l%uf,aL .Klfha j;auka w.h tys PCB ys
    iq/fla'

    This ensures that when the web browser resumes, it can continue execution exactly where it left off.
    fjí n%jqirh kej; wdrïN jQ úg" th k;r l< ia:dkkfhkau l%shd;aul lsÍu È.gu lrf.k hd
    yels nj fuu.ska iy;sl lrhs'

    Loading the program counter of the spreadsheet process:
    me;=reïm;a l%shdj,sfha l%uf,aL .Klh mQrKh lsÍu(

    The program counter value stored in the PCB of the spreadsheet process is loaded into the CPU.
    me;=reïm;a l%shdj,sfha PCB ys .nvd lr we;s l%uf,aL .Klfha w.h CPU fj; mQrKh lrkq ,efí'
    This allows the spreadsheet process to start execution from where it was paused.
    fuh me;=reïm;a l%shdj,shg k;r l< ;ekska l%shd;aul lsÍu wdrïN lsÍug bv i,ihs'

    Purpose / wruqK(
    The program counter ensures that processes can be paused and resumed seamlessly without losing their
    execution state, enabling multitasking in the operating system.
    l%uf,aL .Klh uÕska fufyhqï moaO;sh ;=< nyqld¾hh il%Sh lrñka l%shdj,s j, l%shd;aul ùfï
    ;;a;ajh ke;s fkdù ndOdjlska f;drj ;djld,slj kj;d ;nd kej; wdrïN l< yels nj iy;sl
    lrhs'

 (d) A computer uses 16-bit virtual addresses. This computer has a 32 KB physical memory and a 4 KB page
     size.
     mß.Klhla ìgq 16 l w;:H fhduq Ndú; lrhs' fuu mß.Klhg 32 KB fN!;sl u;lhla we;s
     w;r msgqjl úYd,;ajh 4 KB fõ'

  (i) Write down the number of frames in physical memory.
      fN!;sl u;lfha we;s rduq (frames) .Kk ,shd olajkak'

    There are two main ways to obtain the number of frames in physical memory.
    fN!;sl u;lfha we;s rduq .Kk ,nd .ekSug m%Odk l%u folla ;sfí'

    Method 1:
    The page and frame size in a computer are the same. According to that, this computer’s frame sizes
    should also be 4 KB since a page size is 4 KB.
    mß.Klhl msgq iy rduq m%udKh iudk fõ' tA wkqj msgq m%udKh 4 KB neúka fuu mß.Klfha
    rduq m%udKho 4 KB úh hq;=h'

    To obtain the number of frames, simply divide 32 KB by 4 KB.
    rduq .Kk ,nd .ekSu i|yd" 32 KB" 4 KB lska fnokak'

    The number of frames = 32 / 4 = 8 frames
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                        -112-                                 #ictfromabc


    Method 2:
    We already know that page and frame sizes are equal.
    msgq iy rduq m%udKh iudk nj wms oekgu;a oksuq'

    A virtual and physical address have two parts, a page/frame number and an offset.
    w;:H iy fN!;sl ,smskhl fldgia folla we;" tkï msgq$rduq wxlh iy úia:dmkhhs'

    In order for the page and frame sizes to be the same, the offset part of both virtual and physical addresses
    has to be the same.
    msgqj iy rduq m%udKh iudk ùug kï" w;:H iy fN!;sl ,smsk foflysu úia:dmk fldgi iudk
    úh hq;=h'

    2The total number of bits in a physical address = The total capacity of physical memory
    2The total number of bits in a physical address = 32 KB
    2The total number of bits in a physical address = 25 x 210 B
    The total number of bits in a physical address = 15 bits

    As we can see, there are 15-bits used for a physical address.
    by; fmfkk mßÈ" fN!;sl ,smskhla i|yd Ndú;d lrk ìgq 15 la we;'

    2The number of offset bits = The size of a page/frame
    2The number of offset bits = 4 KB
    2The number of offset bits = 22 x 210 B
    The number of offset bits = 12 bits
    There are 12-bits for the offset part.

    To find out the number of bits used for the page number, we can simply subtract the number of offset
    bits by the total number of bits used in a physical address.
    msgq wxlh i|yd Ndú;d lrk ìgq .Kk fidhd .ekSug" fN!;sl ,smskhl Ndú;d jk uq¿ ìgq
    .Kkska úia:dmk ìgq .Kk wvq l< yelsh'

    The number of bits for the page number = 15 – 12
    The number of bits for the page number = 3 bits

    To find out the number of frames, raise the number of bits used for the page number to the power of 2.
    rduq .Kk fidhd .ekSug" msgq wxlh i|yd Ndú;d lrk ìgq .Kk 2ys n,hg kxjkak'

    The number of frames = 23
    The number of frames = 8

    The method 1 is much easier and takes no more than 10-seconds to find the answer if you knew that
    page and frame sizes are equal.
    m<uq l%uh b;d myiq jk w;r msgq iy rduq m%udKh iudk nj Tn oek isáhfyd;a ms<s;=r fiùug
    ;;amr 10lg jvd .; fkdfõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -113-                                         #ictfromabc


 (ii) A user runs a program having a size of 64 KB on this computer. A few selected fields of the first few rows
      of the page table of that process at a particular time are shown in the figure.
      úYd,;ajh 64 KB jQ l%uf,alLhla mßYS,lfhla fuu mß.Klfha Odjkh lrhs' tla;rd wjia:djl§
      tu l%shdhkfha msgq j.=fõ (page table) uq,a fma<s lsysmfha f;dard.;a lafI;% lsysmhla my; mßÈ fõ'


          Frame      Validity      Notes / igyk(
      0 111          1
                                   •   The page number is used as the index into the page table.
      1 100          1                 msgq wxlh" msgq j.=jg iQplhla (index) f,i Ndú; fõ
      2 110          1
                                   •   The frame number is indicated in binary. Validity bit being 1
      3 101          1                 indicates that the relevant page is in physical memory.
      4 000          0                 rduq wxlh oaúuh f,i olajd we;' ìgqfõ j,x.=;dj ioyd 1
                                       u.ska oelafjkafka tu msgqj fN!;sl u;lfha we;s njhs'
      5 000          0
      6 000          0

     Assume that in the above process the virtual address 0010 0000 0000 0100 is wanted. Write down the 15-
     bit physical address that the above address would get mapped to.
     by; l%shdhkfha 0010 0000 0000 0100 w;:H fhduqj wjYH hehs is;kak' by; fhduqj wkqrEmKh
     jk ìgq 15 lska hq;a fN!;sl fhduqj ,shd olajkak

     Virtual address - 0010 0000 0000 0100

     The first 4-bits are used for the page number. 0010 in binary is 2 in decimal. If we look at the page table,
     the 2nd page’s frame number is 110 and its validity bit is 1. Validity bit being 1 means that this page is
     loaded into the frame 110 in physical memory, therefore, no swap-in needed.
     msgq wxlh i|yd m<uq ìgq 4 Ndú;d fõ' oaúuh 0010 oYu 2 fõ' wm msgq j.=j foi ne¨jfyd;a" 2
     jk msgqfõ rduq wxlh 110 jk w;r tys j,x.= ìgqj 1 fõ' j,x.= ìgqj 1 ùu hkafkka woyia jkafka
     fuu msgqj fN!;sl u;lfha we;s 110 jk rduqj fj; mQrKh ù we;s njhs" tneúka" swap-in wjYH
     fkdfõ'

     Explanation of Page 4 and Its Status / 4 jk msgqfõ meyeÈ,s lsÍu iy tys ;;a;ajh
     Page 4 has a validity bit of 0, which means it is not currently loaded into physical memory. The operating
     system must load this page into memory by assigning a suitable frame. However, Frame 011 (Frame 3) is
     not considered for this page due to the following reasons:

     my; fya;+ka ksid fuu msgqj i|yd 011 jk rduqj ^rduqj 3& i,ld n,kq fkd,efí(

     Reasons Why Frame 011 Cannot Be Allocated / 011 jk rduqj fjka l< fkdyels ùug fya;=

     though it is not visible in the provided subset of the table.
     j.=fõ imhd we;s Wm l=,lfha th fkdfmkqko" 011 jk rduqj oekgu;a msgq j.=fõ ;j;a my< fyda
     ueo we;s msgqjlg mjrd ;sìh yel'

     f,i i,l=Kq lr ;sìh yel'

     •   Avoiding Overwriting Valid Data
         j,x.= o;a; uld ,sùu je<elaùu
     If Frame 011 is currently occupied by another valid page with its validity bit set to 1, the operating
     system cannot reuse it without overwriting critical data.

 (iv) In addition to the above fields of the page table, a Modified bit may also exist. It will be set to 1 when
      data in a page is changed. Why is that information important for the operating system?
      msgq j.=fõ by; lafIa;%j,g wu;rj" ‘fjkiaùï (modified)’ ìgqjla o ;sfnkq we;' msgqfõ o;a;
      fjkia jQ úg tu ìgqj 1 njg m;a flf¾' tu f;dr;=r fufyhqï moaO;shg jeo.;a jkafka wehs@

     The Modified bit (also called the Dirty bit) is essential for the operating system because it indicates
     whether the data in a page has been changed (modified) while it is loaded in memory.
     Modified bit ^Dirty bit f,io ye¢kafõ& tl fufyhqï moaO;sh i|yd w;HjYH fõ" ukao th msgqjl
     o;a; u;lhg mQrKh lr we;s úg th fjkia ù ;sfío hkak fmkakqï lrhs'

     Avoids Unnecessary Writes to Disk / ;eáhg wkjYH f,i ,sùfuka j<lS'
     If the Modified bit is 1, it means the page has been changed and must be written back to disk before
     being replaced or removed from memory.
     Modified bit tl 1 kï" thska woyia jkafka msgqj fjkia lr we;s w;r th m%;sia:dmkh lsÍug
     fyda u;lfhka bj;a lsÍug fmr ;eáhg kej; ,súh hq;= njhs'

     If the Modified bit is 0, the page has not been changed, and there is no need to write it back to disk. This
     saves time and resources.
     Modified bit tl 0 kï" msgqj fjkia lr fkdue;s w;r" th kej; ;eáhg ,sùug wjYH fkdfõ'
     fuh ld,h yd iïm;a b;sß lrhs'

     Optimizes Memory Replacement / u;l wdfoaYkh m%Yia; lrhs
     During page replacement, the operating system can prioritize replacing pages with a Modified bit of 0
     because these pages do not require a write-back to disk. This speeds up the replacement process.
     msgq m%;sia:dmkfha§" fuu msgq j,g ;eáhg wdmiq ,sùula wjYH fkdjk ksid" Modified bit tl 0 jk
     msgq m%;sia:dmkh lsÍug fufyhqï moaO;shg m%uqL;ajh Èh yel' fuh m%;sia:dmk l%shdj,sh fõ.j;a
     lrhs'

     For pages with the Modified bit set to 1, the system ensures the updated data is saved before eviction.
     Modified bit tl 1 f,i ilid we;s msgq i|yd" bj;a lsÍug fmr hdj;ald,Sk l< o;a; iq/lsk nj
     moaO;sh u.ska iy;sl lrhs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                  -115-                                           #ictfromabc


      Ensures Data Consistency / o;a; wkql+,;dj iy;sl lrhs
      The Modified bit helps the operating system maintain consistency between physical memory and the
      storage device (e.g., disk). Without this mechanism, changes made to pages could be lost during page
      replacement.
      fN!;sl u;lh iy .nvd Wmdx.h ^Wod( ;eáh& w;r wkql+,;dj mj;ajd .ekSug fufyhqï moaO;shg
      Wmldr lrhs' fuu hdka;%Kh fkdue;sj" msgq m%;sia:dmkfha§ msgq j,g lrk ,o fjkialï ke;s ù hd
      yel'

      Improves System Efficiency / moaO;s ld¾hlaIu;dj jeä ÈhqKq lrhs
      Tracking the Modified bit reduces unnecessary disk I/O operations, improving the overall performance of
      the system.
      ¨yqne£u u.ska wkjYH ;eá ^I$O& fufyhqï wvq lrñka moaO;sfha iuia; l%shdldß;ajh jeä ÈhqKq
      lrhs'

    Contiguous Allocation / hdno úNckh
    In contiguous allocation, a file’s blocks are stored in adjacent blocks on the disk, requiring only the starting
    block and the file length for access. This method ensures fast sequential access but can lead to challenges
    such as external fragmentation and difficulty accommodating file growth without relocation.
    hdno úNckfha§" f.dkqjl ldKav ;eáfha hdno ldKav j, .nvd lr we;s w;r" m%fõYh i|yd
    wdrïNl ldKavh iy f.dkqfõ È. muKla wjYH fõ' fuu l%uh fõ.j;a wkql%ñl m%fõYhla iy;sl
    lrk kuq;a ndysr LKavkh iy kej; ia:dk.; lsÍulska f;drj f.dkq j¾Okhg bv ie,iSfï
    ÿIalr;d jeks wNsfhda.j,g ;=vq Èh yelsh'

    Linked Allocation / ineÈ úNckh
    In linked allocation, each block of a file contains a pointer to the next block, allowing the file’s data blocks
    to be stored non-contiguously on the disk. This method avoids fragmentation and enables dynamic file
    growth, but accessing the file requires sequential traversal of all pointers, which can slow down access.
    ineÈ úNckfha§" f.dkqjl iEu ldKavhlu B<Õ ldKavh fj; fhduqjla wvx.= jk w;r" tu.ska
    f.dkqfõ o;a; ldKav ;eáfha hdno fkdjk wdldrfhka .nvd lsÍug bv i,ihs' fuu l%uh LKavkh
    ùu j<lajk w;r .;sl f.dkq j¾Okh il%Sh lrhs" kuq;a f.dkqjg m%fõY ùu i|yd ukao.dó l%uhla
    jk ish¨u fhduq yryd isÿ jk wkql%ñl .uka lsÍu wjYH fõ'

      fragmentation of a disk?

      (external fragmentation) we;s lsÍug bv we;af;a l=ulska o@

    Contiguous allocation can cause external fragmentation, while indexed allocation avoids it by allowing
    blocks to be allocated non-contiguously.

    fjka lsÍug bv §fuka th j<lajhs'
